SIX-ROUND SCORECARD FOR THE PLAN FOLD, for calibration. Rounds 1 to 4 plus one authorised verification round after escalation, and one supplementary human-authorised deletion pass that was not a round. VALID FINDINGS 14, 10, 8, 7, 0 (accepted residuals excluded from the first two, which were 15 and 11 raw). SEVERITY CEILING high, medium, low, low, none, falling monotonically and never rising. 0 dismissed at high or critical across the whole loop, so THE BACKSTOP NEVER FIRED. 39 valid findings total. The mechanism, the three-increment division, the ordering argument and the risk classifications survived all six rounds UNTOUCHED; every finding from round 3 onward was editorial. THE DIAGNOSED CAUSE OF THE LENGTH, which differs from step 92's: this document was assembled IN LAYERS across two planner passes, a design pass and four fix passes, and it states the same counts in multiple places, so its characteristic defect is AN AGGREGATE CONTRADICTING A DETAIL ELSEWHERE IN THE SAME DOCUMENT. That class is invisible to a reviewer checking claims against the CODE, which is where a citation points, and it took until round 4 to point a lens at the document against ITSELF. THE TRANSFERABLE RULE: brief a consistency lens EARLY on any artifact assembled in layers, and do not wait for the class to declare itself three times first.

WHAT THIS LOOP CONTRIBUTED TO THE WORKFLOW ITSELF, beyond the artifact. (1) THE TENSE RULE, which paid on its first outing and is the most transferable result: a plan is a set of claims about what WILL exist, so evaluating them the way you evaluate claims about the current tree systematically passes exactly the sentences a plan exists to get right. (2) PER-REVIEWER FINDING-ID PREFIXES, after round 2's two reviewers both used `R2-1` for different findings; zero collisions in rounds 3 and 4. (3) THE MIRROR OF THE ESCAPE VALVE: a triager's site count is a MEASUREMENT, not an instruction, and a producer finding it short should widen and report rather than apply a fix that manufactures a contradiction. Used correctly once, and confirmed by an independent reviewer. (4) THE BOUNDED PRE-EMPTION PRECEDENT recorded above, with its three conditions and the explicit instruction to run the round instead when any is absent. (5) The observation, from a verification lens declining to raise a candidate on the ground that repairing it would author prose, that THIS PROJECT'S CALIBRATION DATA NOW FEEDS BACK INTO REVIEWER JUDGEMENT rather than only into orchestrator briefs.

ACCEPTED RESIDUALS AND OUT-OF-SCOPE ITEMS CARRIED FORWARD, since the review files are commit-deleted and these live here now. (1) `INC2-7` (round 2): no precedence rule for an over-determined `no_active_loop_reason`; do not re-raise. (2) `F-5` (round 1): the `validation-constraints` step is cited as a fold destination in both the sidecar and the TOML's `Q-55` text and NO SUCH STEP EXISTS; accepted as pre-existing rather than a defect of this fold, and THE RIGHT ACTION IS ENTERING THAT STEP, NOT DELETING THE CITATIONS, which are load-bearing for the queue. THE ORCHESTRATOR OWNS RAISING IT; THE PLANNER OWNS AUTHORING IT. (3) THE PLAN TOML'S STEP `title` IS NOT PROJECTED INTO THE RENDERED VIEW AT ALL, ruled a pre-existing property of the tool and out of scope for this fold by both a reviewer and the triager, each verifying it against `src/plan/render.rs` independently; the rendered heading comes from the sidecar's own `###` line and the step table carries only slug and status, so the TOML title and the sidecar heading ALREADY DIVERGE with nothing keeping them in step. That is an unguarded drift surface and belongs in the backlog. (4) `:170`'s "all four surfaces" beside a quoted receipt's "all three": ruled NOT A DEFECT by both verification lenses, two true statements on different counting bases; do not reopen.

A FOURTH `agent-scaffold next` DEFECT, FOUND BY RUNNING THE TOOL WHILE ANSWERING A HUMAN QUESTION ABOUT REMAINING WORK (2026-08-01), and it belongs to the validation-constraints step with the other three. EVERY ONE OF THE 95 STEPS HAS `blocked_by = []`: `grep -c 'blocked_by = \[\]'` returns 95 and `grep -cE 'blocked_by = \[".+'` returns 0, so the field is unused project-wide. THE CONSEQUENCE: the entire ordering to the rename lives in LEDGER PROSE and in the human's standing pause instruction, and NOTHING in the structured source encodes it, including `rename-to-agent-flow` itself (order 84, `deferred`, `blocked_by = []`). Run on main at `9e5df0b`, `next --source docs/plans/agent-scaffold.plan.toml` therefore advises "spawn a reviewer for the first review round" on `workflow-calibration`, which is not the next action. THIS IS THE SAME CLASS AS THE OTHER THREE, not a new one: the structured source is INCOMPLETE relative to the prose and the tool answers confidently from the incomplete half, exactly as it did when it reported `workflow-driver` complete over committed unbuilt Stage 2 work. Recorded here rather than fixed because populating `blocked_by` is PLAN CONTENT and so planner work, outside the orchestrator's four direct-on-main edits.

HUMAN DECISION (2026-08-01) ON WHAT TO DO NEXT: BUILD `workflow-enforcement-tier` INC1 NOW, folding the `blocked_by` gap into the validation-constraints step when that is authored. Chosen over authoring validation-constraints first, over a small standalone `blocked_by` step, and over clearing all plan debt in one planner pass. THE REASONING PRESENTED AND ACCEPTED: the `Q-55-mechanism` decision already sequenced enforcement-tier AHEAD of validation-constraints on measured evidence, so authoring validation-constraints first would REVERSE a sequencing decided with the design pass in hand; the gap is ADVISORY-ONLY and the ledger's RESUME STATE durably carries the real sequence, which is the artifact the resume protocol reads first, so the failure mode is bounded by the mechanism designed to bound it; and a fourth instance of an existing defect family belongs in the step that already owns that family rather than in a step of its own (Minimal by default, Ground decisions in evidence, Structured data first, project for humans). THE ACCEPTED COST, stated at the time: `next` keeps giving wrong advice for the whole enforcement-tier build.

STEP 94 `workflow-enforcement-tier` IS IN PROGRESS (status flipped on main at `69c0525`, an orchestrator direct-on-main edit within the closed list). A FULL TASK-ENTRY BRIEF WAS PUT TO THE HUMAN AND ANSWERED "go as briefed", over adding a dedicated adversarial pass before the review rounds and over holding for more briefing. INC1 IS BUILT at `f491c4e` on branch `impl/enforcement-inc1` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/impl-enforcement-inc1`), UNMERGED, and its work review is open. The diff is `src/main.rs` +153/-31, a new 473-line test file `tests/metrics_and_ledger_anchor_to_the_plan_source.rs` with 9 tests, one README paragraph and one CHANGELOG line. Verified by the orchestrator on the branch rather than from the report: tree clean, 395 tests passing across eight binaries with 0 failures (386 baseline plus the 9 new), ASCII counts 0 on all four changed files.

THE IMPLEMENTER'S RED-THEN-GREEN EVIDENCE IS THE REAL KIND, and its shape is worth copying. It wrote the tests first, `git stash push`ed ONLY `src/main.rs`, ran the new test binary against pre-change code, and reported the ACTUAL failure text per test: 7 of 9 failed. THE OTHER TWO PASSED PRE-CHANGE BY DESIGN and are the more interesting half, being PINS that fail if a later change "improves" what they protect: one holds check 9's byte-identity property, the other holds accepted cost (i) so a future canonicalising default trips over it. It also ran 37 SELF-ATTACKS and reported every negative, which the brief required because the design pass measured this defect class as SELF-CONCEALING (the explorer's own build was correct on the reported reproduction and still printed `workflow invariants hold` at exit 0 on two other inputs until it attacked itself deliberately).

THE ONE SUBSTANTIVE DISCLOSURE, MADE BY THE IMPLEMENTER AND REPRODUCED FIRST-HAND BY THE ORCHESTRATOR RATHER THAN RELAYED: a `..` that ESCAPES a `docs/plans` directory makes the lexical walk find THAT `docs/plans` and derive the wrong project's root. Measured with distinct record counts so the printed count identifies the file actually read: `--source <F>/trap/other/p.plan.toml` reads `trap/other`'s 5-record log and is correct, while `--source <F>/trap/docs/plans/../../other/p.plan.toml`, THE SAME FILE, reads `trap`'s 1-record log and prints `workflow invariants hold` at exit 0. NOT A REGRESSION: pre-change BOTH spellings gave a false green from the CWD's log, so inc1 shrinks the false-green surface here rather than adding to it. The implementer did not change the derivation, on three grounds it recorded: the purely-lexical rule with no normalisation is a settled decision, lexical `..` normalisation is unsound across symlinks (the class `is_safe_sidecar_ref` exists to guard), and inc2's canonical guard closes it unchanged because the guard's root comes from the source's CANONICALISED location.

WHY THAT DISCLOSURE MATTERS MORE FOR THE PLAN THAN FOR THE CODE, and it is carried to the reviewers as a plan question: the sidecar justifies the `..` behaviour by saying `Path::file_name` returns `None` for a `..` component "so the walk skips past it and still finds the real `docs/plans` above". IN THIS CASE THAT SAME MECHANISM FINDS THE WRONG ONE. So a just-converged document contains a justification NARROWER THAN IT READS, because the design pass's case matrix constructed a `..` INSIDE a path and never one ESCAPING a `docs/plans`. A six-round plan review does not make a claim true of cases nobody built.

WORK REVIEW ROUND 1 IS RUNNING with two lenses from the branch tip `f491c4e`: adversarial correctness (attack the derivation, rule on the `..` disclosure and on the sidecar's claim) and fidelity plus scope plus the acceptance checks. THE FIDELITY LENS RETURNED CLEAN, ZERO FINDINGS, and it verified rather than accepted: it re-derived the acceptance-check partition itself and confirmed it correct IN BOTH DIRECTIONS (nothing deferred that should have shipped, nothing built early), BUILT A BINARY FROM THE PRE-FIX COMMIT `69c0525` and reproduced each defect against it before confirming the fix, used `cmp` rather than `diff` for check 9's byte-identity on stdout AND stderr, and verified the CHANGELOG precedent through the full git history (a `### Fixed` subsection has never existed in that file). All four settled decisions confirmed by `file:line`; no refusal, containment predicate or canonical root derivation built early.

THE ADVERSARIAL LENS RAISED 3 FINDINGS (1 medium, 2 low) AND ZERO MECHANISM DEFECTS, so round 1 is NOT clean, streak 0 of 2, triage owed. ITS RIGOUR SET A BAR WORTH KEEPING: 51 attacks across path spellings, layout edges, symlinks, explicit flag values, the Markdown substrate, the no-anchor case, error inputs, ledger resolution and the JSON surfaces, AND EVERY ATTACK WAS ALSO RUN AGAINST THE PRE-CHANGE BINARY, so "no input got worse" is MEASURED rather than assumed. It re-established the implementer's own evidence rather than trusting it, building `69c0525` in a separate clone to confirm the 7-red-2-pin split. IT RULED THE `..` DISCLOSURE NOT A FINDING AGAINST THE IMPLEMENTATION on four checked grounds, and it STRENGTHENED the demonstration beyond the orchestrator's: the same file under two spellings gives `state: awaiting-first-review` on one and `state: converged`, `streak: 1/1`, `next: mark the step complete, re-render, and commit` at exit 0 on the other, so the defect reaches the AGENT-FACING surface and not just the validator.

BUT IT FOUND THE CLAIMS ABOUT THAT BEHAVIOUR DEFECTIVE IN TWO PLACES, AND THE DIAGNOSIS IS THE USEFUL PART. `W1A-1` (medium, `src/main.rs:1168-1170`): the `project_root_of_source` doc comment says a `..` is skipped "and the real `docs/plans` above it still matches", which is measured false for a `..` that ESCAPES a `docs/plans`. THE BEHAVIOUR IS ACCEPTABLE AND THE CLAIM IS THE DEFECT, because it tells a later reader the inc2 guard is REDUNDANT for exactly the case that produces a self-concealing false green. `W1A-2` (low): the same over-broad claim in the SIDECAR at line 162, inside a list of what the design pass measured, which is the document inc2's implementer will read as authority. `W1A-3` (low): the new test file's module doc claims four pins "including the two no-anchor cases" where the file has ONE no-anchor run, and `default_ledger_path`'s no-anchor arm, which inc1 specifies, IS PINNED BY NOTHING IN THE SUITE. That last is a genuine COVERAGE gap rather than a wording one.

A ROUTING QUESTION THIS ROUND RAISES FOR THE FIX PASS, recorded because it is new: the findings SPAN TWO ROLES. `W1A-1` and `W1A-3` are code and tests and route to the IMPLEMENTER; `W1A-2` is sidecar prose, which is reviewed product content and routes to the PLANNER by the precedent set at the step-92 close. A single work-review round has not previously produced findings owned by two different writers, and the orchestrator must not fold either one itself.

THE TRIAGE RULED ALL THREE VALID (1 medium, 2 low, 0 dismissed, 0 accept-residual) AND SPLIT `W1A-3` INTO TWO INDEPENDENTLY-OWED HALVES, a wrong count and an untested arm. ITS EVIDENCE WORK RAISED THE BAR IN THREE WAYS WORTH COPYING. (1) IT PROVED THE COVERAGE GAP BY MUTATION rather than by grep: changing `default_ledger_path`'s no-anchor arm to a `MUTANT` path in a copy OUTSIDE the repo left the whole suite green at 395 passed, which upgrades "I could not find a test" to "no test exists". (2) IT MEASURED THE REVIEWER'S PROPOSED FIX AS WRONG, finding a THIRD false claim in the same sentence the reviewer had not named, so the reviewer's numeral-only edit would have re-opened the finding in round 2. (3) IT CAUGHT ITS OWN CONTAMINATED MEASUREMENT: a stale test binary with `CARGO_BIN_EXE` baked to another tree made its first pre-change run report 9 of 9 green, and it forced a recompile, reproduced the true 2-passed-7-failed split, and WROTE THE CONTAMINATION INTO THE TRIAGE FILE AS A TRAP for later rounds. It also found a twin the reviewer's scope missed (`docs/plans/agent-scaffold.md:1557` carries the sidecar sentence VERBATIM, so `W1A-2` is 2 sites not 1, the second closed by regeneration) and DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED a third occurrence in a dated explorer record, recording the exclusion so a later round does not re-file it.

THE ROUTING RULING: implementer takes `W1A-1` and both halves of `W1A-3` (`src/` and `tests/` only); planner takes `W1A-2` (sidecar plus regeneration, both files in one commit). No fix requires either writer to touch the other's artifact, they proceed IN PARALLEL with no ordering constraint, and the only coupling, that both state the same corrected fact, is discharged by the triage SUPPLYING EXACT WORDING rather than by sequencing. The one hazard it flagged: a sidecar edit landing without the re-render turns `render --check` red and opens round 2 with a self-inflicted finding.

BOTH FIX PASSES LANDED AND ARE MERGED INTO THE INCREMENT at `f8f2e09` (implementer `be2c897`, planner `f2850d3` rebased to `f8f2e09`). The two lanes were verified DISJOINT by file list before merging. BOTH WRITERS AUTHORED ZERO WORDS: every prose change in both lanes is verbatim triage text, which the planner verified MECHANICALLY as exact substrings (114 words copied, 0 authored) and the implementer enumerated clause by clause. Post-merge on the branch: 395 passed / 0 failed across eight binaries, clippy silent, `render --check` up to date, ASCII 0 on all four changed files.

THE IMPLEMENTER'S MUTATION EVIDENCE IS THE STRONGEST ARTIFACT OF THIS ROUND. It reproduced the triager's mutation in a scratch copy outside any repo, HANDLED THE CONTAMINATION TRAP EXPLICITLY by checking the embedded `CARGO_BIN_EXE` with `strings` to prove the binary under test was the mutant (0 hits for the worktree path), and got EXACTLY ONE FAILURE ACROSS THE WHOLE SUITE, which independently reconfirms that nothing else pinned that arm and establishes its new assertion as the pin. It then restored and diffed to confirm the worktree was never mutated. NOTE FOR FUTURE ROUNDS: the suite total stayed 395 rather than moving to 396, correctly, because the assertion lives INSIDE an existing `#[test]` and libtest counts test functions rather than assertions; a reviewer expecting 396 would be wrong.

TWO PRODUCER DISCLOSURES CARRIED INTO ROUND 2 RATHER THAN FIXED, both reported rather than silently improved. (i) FROM THE PLANNER, and it is the same species as the finding it closes: the triager's supplied narrowing says the matrix covered "a `..` that stays below the project's own `docs/plans`", but the actual measured non-escaping fixture `away/sub/../docs/plans/p.plan.toml` puts the `..` ABOVE `docs/plans`, so the sentence now UNDERSTATES what was measured. The fix for "too broad" landed slightly "too narrow". (ii) FROM THE IMPLEMENTER: the doc comment on `plain_validate_and_a_sourceless_run_keep_their_behaviour` enumerates the test's cases and does not name the third run the new assertion adds; it is INCOMPLETE rather than wrong, it asserts no exclusivity, and the triage had explicitly ruled that line accurate and to leave it alone.

WHY THE ORCHESTRATOR DID NOT CLOSE DISCLOSURE (i), stated because the last such call went the other way and the difference must be legible: the bounded pre-emption precedent recorded above has THREE conditions, and the second is plainly absent here since NO TRIAGER HAS RULED ON THIS WORDING AT ALL. The recorded answer when any condition is absent is to run the round. Following the rule the first time it is inconvenient is the whole point of having written it down; understating a measurement leaves nothing false, so the worst case is one `low` finding in a round that has to establish itself clean regardless. ROUND 2's RESIDUE LENS LATER RULED DISCLOSURE (i) NOT A DEFECT, on a precedent the orchestrator had not looked for: `:320` already uses "stays INSIDE the root" against "climbs out" for containment boundaries, so the idiom is the document's own and the sentence holds under either reading. The orchestrator's reading was wrong and the decision to carry it rather than fix it was right anyway.

WORK REVIEW ROUND 1 (record 246, `new_valid`, streak 0 of 2): two lenses, ADVERSARIAL CORRECTNESS (`claude-opus-5`, 3 findings) and FIDELITY plus SCOPE plus the acceptance checks (`claude-sonnet-5`, CLEAN). 3 raw, 3 valid, 0 dismissed, 1 medium and 2 low, ZERO MECHANISM DEFECTS. The adversarial lens ran 51 ATTACKS AND RAN EVERY ONE AGAINST THE PRE-CHANGE BINARY TOO, so "no input got worse" is measured. The fidelity lens re-derived the acceptance-check partition itself and confirmed it correct IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, then BUILT A BINARY FROM `69c0525` and reproduced each defect before confirming its fix, used `cmp` rather than `diff` for check 9, and verified the CHANGELOG precedent through the full git history.

WORK REVIEW ROUND 2 (record 247, `new_valid`, streak still 0 of 2): the RESIDUE lens (`claude-sonnet-5`) returned CLEAN and ruled both producer disclosures not defects, while a CLAIMS-VERSUS-BEHAVIOUR lens (`claude-opus-5`) built an 81-CLAIM INVENTORY, tested each BY RUNNING IT, and returned 4 findings from 12 failures: 1 high, 2 medium, 1 low.

THE LENS-SELECTION LESSON, WHICH IS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE RESULT OF THIS INCREMENT. Round 1's adversarial lens ran 51 attacks across path spellings, layouts, symlinks, substrates and error inputs and found ZERO mechanism defects. Round 2's claims lens found a MECHANISM defect by a completely different route: it took the comment sitting directly above the resolution code, which read "Resolving from the plan rather than from the process working directory is what stops a plan being joined to an unrelated project's log", and TESTED THAT SENTENCE. The sentence was false and the falsifying case was the defect. ADVERSARIAL AND CLAIMS-BASED LENSES ARE NOT SUBSTITUTES: one asks what inputs break the code, the other asks whether what the code says about itself is true, and on this artifact the second found what the first missed. It is the second time the read-the-claims approach beat the attack-the-code approach here, the first being the plan review's consistency lens.

`W2B-1` WAS RAISED AS A REGRESSION AND THE TRIAGER REFUTED THAT, which is the round's most important correction. WORK REVIEW ROUND 3 (record 248, `new_valid`, streak still 0 of 2): 7 raw, 7 valid, 0 dismissed, ONE RULED OUT OF SCOPE leaving 6 in scope (2 medium, 4 low). THE OUT-OF-SCOPE PRECEDENT, SET BY THE ROUND 3 TRIAGER AND BINDING ON LATER LOOPS: A VALID FINDING THAT IS OUT OF SCOPE FOR THE INCREMENT DOES NOT RESET THE CONVERGENCE STREAK. The streak is evidence about ONE ARTIFACT, not a tally of defects found while reading; a defect predating the increment carries no information about whether the increment has stopped yielding defects, and counting it makes convergence a function of repo-wide defect density that no work on the increment can reduce. FOUR CONDITIONS, ALL REQUIRED: (1) provenance predates the base commit, (2) no commit in range modifies the claim's lines, (3) INDEPENDENT SUBJECT, meaning the claim is not about what the increment changed AND the increment's change is not what falsified it, and (4) no shared fix. CONDITION 3 DOES THE REAL WORK: a stale claim THE INCREMENT'S OWN CHANGE BROKE is in scope regardless of authorship. Guards: triager-binding with commands quoted, the minimal fix recorded anyway, totals must report the category explicitly rather than as a bare "clean", and reviewers may re-raise next round. THE TRIAGER SET IT IN THE ONE CIRCUMSTANCE WHERE THE RULING COULD NOT BUY THE OUTCOME (six in-scope findings decided the round regardless) and RECORDED A FACT AGAINST ITS OWN RULING (inc1 did edit the same doc block ten lines above the claim; lines untouched, so condition 2 holds, and adjacency is not authorship). That is how a precedent should be set.

THE TRIAGER ALSO CAUGHT A FALSE CONFIRMATION IN THE REVIEWER'S EVIDENCE, which is a trap worth naming: `W3B-4`'s reproduction works only if a particular directory exists, and WITHOUT it the command prints `no log found`, which COINCIDENTALLY MATCHES what the doc predicts, so an unwary re-run reads as confirmation of the opposite conclusion. It found a spelling that falsifies without the synthetic directory. It further established that `W3B-1` and `W3B-5` MUST LAND AS ONE EDIT, because `W3B-5` exists precisely because round 2's qualifier insertion made an adjacent true clause false, and `W3B-1` inserts that identical qualifier at that identical clause elsewhere.

A DELETED CLAIM CANNOT BE FALSIFIED AT AN EDGE; that is the whole point.

ORCHESTRATOR DEFECT (11): THE ORCHESTRATOR RAN BARE `cargo clippy` OUTSIDE THE NIX ENVIRONMENT and got 169 errors including `found crate toml compiled by an incompatible version of rustc`, then began reporting it before recognising the signature. The justfile exists precisely to prevent this: `just clippy` wraps the direnv prefix. THE CURE: never invoke `cargo` directly; use `just <recipe>`, or `direnv exec . cargo ...` when a recipe does not cover the flags needed (`just clippy` omits `-D warnings`, so the strict form must be run as `direnv exec . cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`). A wall of `E0514 incompatible version of rustc` is diagnostic of the wrong toolchain, not of the code.

DESIGN PASS OPENED (2026-07-31) ON THE METRICS-PATH MECHANISM, three worktree-isolated explorers dispatched, records owed to `docs/plans/workflow-enforcement-tier.explorations/`. THE SESSION WAS RESUMED, state reconstructed from `AGENTS.md` plus the plan's Status line plus this anchor, and the preflight run and CONFIRMED BY THE HUMAN as stated (tier 2, git worktree, re-verified: neither `agent-box` nor `agent-images` on PATH and `.agents/isolation-guidance.md` absent; report-and-continue cadence). The human also chose the three-explorer build-and-measure dispatch over two-explorer and single-explorer options. The branch is one docs-only commit ahead of main, so the code under test is identical to main's. THE MANDATES ARE DELIBERATELY ASYMMETRIC: explorer A (`explore/metrics-path-a`, `claude-opus-5`) builds candidate (a), anchor-to-source-root, and must resolve its two named sub-questions BY BUILDING (defaulted-versus-explicit `--metrics`, and a source not under `docs/plans/`); explorer B (`explore/metrics-path-b`, `claude-opus-5`) builds candidate (d), the `[meta]`-declared log path, and must QUANTIFY the migration cost the sidecar objects to and attack its own candidate with a plan that declares a foreign path; explorer C (`explore/metrics-path-c`, `claude-sonnet-5`) maps the space INDEPENDENTLY before reading the sidecar's candidate list, builds whatever it judges best, then attacks (a) and (d) by construction. A and B share a model SO THE COMPARISON BETWEEN THE TWO SERIOUS CONTENDERS IS MODEL-FAIR, which matters because the sidecar already argues (d) is where the cleaner architecture points and a weaker model on (d) would have biased the comparison toward (a); C differs by model per the reviewer-diversity discipline, and its mandate is the one that most rewards a different prior.

THE DESIGN PASS IS DONE (2026-07-31) AND ALL THREE RECORDS ARE ON MAIN at `docs/plans/workflow-enforcement-tier.explorations/` (1514 lines: `metrics-path-anchor-to-source.md` 521, `metrics-path-independent-map.md` 510, `metrics-path-plan-declared.md` 483). Each explorer honoured the output contract exactly, committing ONE file and leaving its build uncommitted; the three builds are preserved as `git stash` entries 0 through 2, named by explorer and finding, and the three `explore/metrics-path-{a,b,c}` branches are kept while their worktrees are removed. All three ran 386 tests passing and clippy clean under `-D warnings`.

THE HEADLINE: BOTH BUILDERS ARGUED AGAINST THE CANDIDATE THEY WERE TOLD TO BUILD, which is the outcome the build-and-measure mandate exists to make reachable and which a paper design pass could not have produced. CANDIDATE (d), THE `[meta]`-DECLARED LOG PATH, IS REJECTED, by its own builder and independently by the adversary. B measured that the declared field contributes NOTHING on shipping day, running the borrowed-slug fixture under both absent-field policies against one build with a byte-identical plan, and every plan in existence omits the field (measured at zero occurrences in this repo's plan and in the scaffolded template), so 100 percent of (d)'s effect comes from the changed DEFAULT, which is candidate (a). Worse, the field is a new way to write the defect down PERMANENTLY: a plan declaring `metrics = "../../../../docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl"` reconstructs the full false pass, now CWD-INDEPENDENT so it reproduces from inside the fixture too, committed to the repo, and passing `validate --source` clean. The validator CANNOT refuse it, because the conventional log lives outside the plan directory so the source-relative default needs `..` itself, which is the exact component `is_safe_sidecar_ref` exists to forbid; that is a LAYOUT fact, not an implementation choice. C reached the same vulnerability independently as its attack 3, citing the same in-repo precedent.

CANDIDATE (a) IS THE RIGHT BASE AND IS INSUFFICIENT ALONE, established by A ATTACKING ITS OWN BUILD after finishing it. A produced TWO FURTHER FALSE PASSES against the fixed build, one with an explicit RELATIVE `--metrics` and one through a SYMLINKED plan source, both printing `workflow invariants hold` at exit 0 for a foreign project with no review evidence. A's framing is the load-bearing part and should survive into the step: shipping (a) alone closes the KNOWN REPRODUCTION and leaves the DEFECT CLASS open, which is the worst available outcome because the next reader will believe it fixed. C reached the same conclusion by a different route, its attack 4 finding that an explicit-but-relative `--metrics` bypasses (a) AND (d) alike since both are scoped only to the default case. THAT IS THE PASS'S STRONGEST CONVERGENCE: two explorers, different mandates and different models, independently found the same bypass.

SUB-QUESTION 1 IS SETTLED BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN PREFERENCE: `Option<PathBuf>` beats clap's `ArgMatches::value_source`, being 17 lines smaller, printing no now-false `[default:]` in `--help`, and carrying no stringly-typed lookup. A DEMONSTRATED the `value_source` failure mode by renaming the arg id, and the asymmetry is the argument: debug PANICS at exit 101 while RELEASE SILENTLY IGNORES the user's `--metrics` and reads a different file. That is the same self-concealing class as the defect under repair, which is what makes it disqualifying rather than merely uglier, and it independently vindicates the sidecar's "Make illegal states unrepresentable" reasoning that A was told to test rather than repeat. SUB-QUESTION 2 IS ANSWERED as a lexical nearest-wins upward search for a `docs/plans` ancestor with fallback to the source's own directory, measured over a matrix where each candidate log carries a distinct record count; the `--source myplan.plan.toml`-at-a-root case the sidecar flagged as conventionless resolves correctly through the fallback. TWO CASES STILL GO WRONG AND A NAMED THEM RATHER THAN BURYING THEM: a bare filename run from inside `docs/plans` (a silent miss no containment guard can catch, since containment is not correctness), and a SYMLINKED `docs/plans` directory, which becomes a FALSE REFUSAL. A also measured a canonicalising variant that fixes the first but turns every printed metrics path absolute, changing output on the correct case.

THE SIBLING COMMANDS ARE NOT SEPARABLE, AND THE ORCHESTRATOR VERIFIED THIS FIRST-HAND ON MAIN RATHER THAN RELAYING IT. The sidecar's stated ground for separating them is that "a wrong path there yields an empty projection rather than a false assertion". THAT IS FALSE, and both explorers falsified it independently by different mechanisms. Reproduced directly by the orchestrator at `3170e3f` against a scaffolded non-instrumented fixture whose single step slug was set to `triager-runs-only-on-findings` and status `in-progress`, run from the agent-scaffold root: `next --source <foreign plan>` printed `metrics: 235 records`, `state: converged`, `streak: 1/1`, `rounds: 2/5`, `next: mark the step complete, re-render, and commit`, and the summary line `step ... converged (streak 1/1); mark the step complete, re-render, and commit.`, at EXIT 0, for a project with ZERO rounds of its own. B separately reproduced `status --resume` printing THIS repository's entire RESUME STATE verbatim, branch names and in-flight review state included, into a foreign project's agent brief. SO THE PROJECTIONS ARE ARGUABLY THE MORE URGENT HALF: `validate`'s false green misleads a HUMAN who may not be looking, while `next`'s false instruction is consumed by an AGENT that acts on it, and `status --resume` is content injection into an instruction rather than a wrong boolean. The human had already added the siblings to scope before this evidence existed; the evidence now bears on their ORDER, not merely their inclusion.

THE ONE DIRECTION NONE OF THE FOUR CANDIDATES TOOK, BUILT BY C: PROJECT IDENTITY IN THE ROUND RECORD ITSELF, an optional `project` field on `Round` and on the plan's `[meta]`, filtering the join in `check_workflow_toml`. It is the ONLY mechanism measured that separates two projects LEGITIMATELY SHARING ONE MERGED LOG, which no path-based mechanism can address, and it is a no-op when undeclared so the correct case is unaffected. C reported its own limitations unprompted: it is OPT-IN, so unmigrated logs and plans stay vulnerable, and a sibling call site at `src/next.rs:1339` is not covered by its minimal-diff filter placement. This is the data-model direction the sidecar already predicted would land in the queued validation-constraints step, now with a working proof rather than a prediction.

THREE CORRECTIONS TO THE SIDECAR, all measured. (i) The render cost of (d) is ZERO, contradicting the brief's stated cost list; `render --check` passes with the field declared, and the real unstated cost is `deny_unknown_fields` turning the field into a hard plan-format version FENCE. (ii) `default_ledger_path` does NOT assume the `<root>/docs/plans/` layout as the sidecar states, it assumes CWD IS THE ROOT and breaks when it is not; A adds that the ledger needs no root derivation at all, since it lives beside the plan. (iii) The siblings-are-separable reasoning is false, per the paragraph above. A fourth item is NOT a correction but a confirmation: a log copied into a project's own `docs/metrics/` still joins by bare slug and greens, so the sidecar is right that every path mechanism fixes the PATH and not the DATA.

THE ONE DIVERGENCE BETWEEN EXPLORERS, LEFT OPEN FOR THE HUMAN RATHER THAN ADJUDICATED BY THE ORCHESTRATOR, is narrow and is about the same failure: A wants candidate (b) added as a CONTAINMENT GUARD layered on top of (a), roughly 80 more lines sharing the same root derivation, on the reasoning that (a) decides WHICH log to read while (b) decides whether the log you were TOLD to read is ALLOWED; C wants the derivation itself to HARD-ERROR rather than fall back when the source is not under a `docs/plans`-shaped root. Both reject the silent CWD fallback, so they agree on the defect and differ on where the refusal lives. C additionally raised, explicitly for the human, whether inc1 should ship (a) alone or (a) plus the two optional identity fields from the start, given the cost is small.

ORCHESTRATOR DEFECTS THIS PASS, recorded like the earlier five so they are not repeated. (6) THE BRIEF ASKED FOR AN IMPOSSIBLE ISOLATION: explorer C was told to read the sidecar's reproduction but SKIP its candidate-list section until after mapping the space independently, which the Read tool cannot do, since it returns the file whole. C DISCLOSED THIS HONESTLY IN ITS RECORD rather than claiming clean isolation, which is the correct behaviour and is why the defect is the orchestrator's alone; but the consequence stands, and C's convergence on all four sidecar candidates MUST NOT be read as independent replication. Its genuinely independent contributions are the ones its list adds BEYOND the four, and its attacks, which are constructions rather than recollections. THE CURE for any future independent-mapping mandate: give the mapper a REDACTED copy of the source document, or have it write and commit its list before it is given the document at all. (7) THE TMPDIR BRIEF COLLIDED WITH THE SUITE: all three explorers were told to point `TMPDIR` at a scratch directory inside their worktree, which is a git repository, and 3 of the 386 tests assert they are NOT inside one. All three hit it and all three correctly isolated it as unrelated to their changes, C routing around it by using the session scratchpad for `cargo test`. No result was corrupted, but the brief cost three agents time on a trap the orchestrator set. THE CURE: brief a scratch `TMPDIR` OUTSIDE any git repository. The collision is also a real property of the suite and is now recorded as such.

TWO HUMAN DECISIONS CLOSED THE DESIGN PASS (2026-07-31), receipts 236 and 237, `q_id` `Q-55-mechanism` and `Q-55-noconvention`, both `task` `workflow-enforcement-tier`, following the receipt precedent this step already set with `Q-55` and `Q-55-scope` rather than registering new `[[question]]` items. (1) SCOPE: ANCHOR PLUS REFUSAL, IDENTITY QUEUED. Ship candidate (a) anchored to the plan source PLUS a refusal for unsafe pairings, covering `validate`, `next`, `status` and the ledger path, and QUEUE project identity to the validation-constraints step. The human declined both the wider option (identity fields in the same step) and the narrower one (anchor only). The reasoning that carried it: the pass measured that anchoring alone leaves the DEFECT CLASS open, so the refusal is not gold-plating; while pulling identity in would edit the record schema that W3, W4 and W5 all read, which the calibration close already argued should be ONE deliberate edit rather than a rider on a path fix (Minimal by default, against Ground decisions in evidence, with the evidence deciding where the line falls). (2) THE CONVENTIONLESS-SOURCE CASE: FALL BACK TO THE SOURCE'S OWN DIRECTORY, explorer A's route, with containment refusal layered on top, over explorer C's hard-error. A had MEASURED the fallback resolving the `--source myplan.plan.toml`-at-a-root case correctly, and Safe on existing projects favours resolving a legitimate layout over rejecting it. THE ACCEPTED COST, which the implementer must not treat as an oversight to fix: a BARE FILENAME run from inside `docs/plans` remains a silent miss, and A established that no containment guard can catch it because containment is not correctness. THE OTHER KNOWN GAP AT THIS CHOICE, also accepted: a symlinked `docs/plans` directory becomes a FALSE REFUSAL under the guard, and A measured that the canonicalising variant which would fix it turns every printed metrics path absolute, changing output on the correct case.

THE SECOND PLANNER PASS RAN (2026-07-31) AND RE-DERIVED THREE INCREMENTS, NOT TWO, on branch `plan/q55-enforcement` (rebased onto main first, base-currency). Its division rule is stated and worth keeping: AN INCREMENT IS A UNIT WHOSE REVIEW ASKS ONE QUESTION. `inc1` is the resolution rule and ALL FOUR call sites (`validate`, `status`, `next`, and both ledger call sites), on the ground that splitting `validate` from the siblings would review the same derivation twice and would leave two commands DISAGREEING about which log belongs to a plan. `inc2` is the containment refusal on `validate --workflow` only, separate because it is the ONLY part that makes a currently-succeeding invocation fail, it carries a KNOWN false positive, and it deliberately runs a canonical resolution against a lexical default so its review must check the SPLIT rather than one rule. `inc3` is the tier policy PLUS the SE-3 documentation half, combined because the pack sentence's truth is a FUNCTION of the tier policy's behaviour, so reviewing the sentence means running the policy's cases. ALL THREE ARE `risky`, argued separately rather than inherited. THE NEW ARGUMENT THE FIRST PASS COULD NOT HAVE MADE is `inc3`'s: it edits the shipped pack and regenerates two drift-guarded files, and its content is A COVERAGE CLAIM ABOUT AN ENFORCEMENT BOUNDARY, which is the one artifact species this project has calibration data on, and THE DATA IS BAD (step 92: six rounds, fifteen findings, all prose, against a project median of two). THE ORDERING ARGUMENT GENERALISED CORRECTLY: the tier policy goes LAST because it is the only increment that turns a green run red, and EVERY escape hatch a user reaches for when it does is closed by an earlier increment, standing somewhere else by `inc1` and passing `--metrics` by hand at a foreign log by `inc2`.

THE PLANNER RAISED A SCOPE QUESTION RATHER THAN DECIDING IT, copying the step-90 precedent, and THE HUMAN CHOSE A THIRD OPTION NEITHER READING CONTAINED (2026-07-31, receipt 238, `q_id` `Q-55-refusalscope`). The question was whether the containment refusal covers `status` and `next` or only `validate --workflow`. THE DECISION: the projections ANCHOR as in the narrow reading, and where the pairing is still unsafe (the case that survives anchoring, an EXPLICIT `--metrics` naming a foreign log) `status` and `next` OMIT THE WORKFLOW-DERIVED FIELDS and say why, EXITING 0. They neither refuse nor project. THE GROUND RESOLVES THE PLANNER'S OWN CONTRACT OBJECTION RATHER THAN OVERRIDING IT, and it came from a passage the planner itself cited: `README.md:226` does not merely promise the projections never fail, it says "Unlike `validate` it never fails on a missing or malformed file (a missing part is simply left out of the projection)", verified by the orchestrator, with `run_resume`'s doc comment at `src/main.rs:1150` matching ("A missing ledger or absent section prints a note and exits 0"). A log that does not belong to this plan IS a part unavailable for this projection, so omitting it is THE DOCUMENTED CONTRACT APPLIED LITERALLY rather than an exception carved into it, which is why this option needed none of the authorisation the wide reading would have required. THE PLANNER'S TWO OPTIONS WERE BOTH DEFENSIBLE AND BOTH WORSE: narrow leaves `next --source <foreign> --metrics <foreign log>` still emitting `state: converged` and instructing an agent to mark a step complete, and wide breaks a documented contract this step was not authorised to change. A CORRECTION OWED TO THE PLANNER'S OWN REPORT, since it may have reached the sidecar: it wrote that anchoring "is what removes the fabricated `next` instruction", but anchoring removes it ONLY for the DEFAULT `--metrics` case, and the explicit-`--metrics` case is precisely what survives, which is the whole reason this decision exists.

THE TMPDIR SUITE DEFECT IS SCHEDULED AS A BACKLOG STEP (human, 2026-07-31), behind the release gates, and it is a SCHEDULING decision against the standing backlog so NO `[[question]]` and NO receipt are owed, on the same precedent as the 2026-07-30 release-gated resequencing. Three of the 386 tests FAIL WHEN `TMPDIR` POINTS INSIDE A GIT REPOSITORY because they assert they are not in one. THE TENSION IS THE ACTUAL DEFECT AND SHOULD BE WRITTEN UP AS SUCH: this project's scratch-`TMPDIR` discipline exists because an earlier agent left 32272 directories in `/tmp`, and following that discipline inside a worktree now breaks the suite, so the two disciplines are in direct conflict. THE EVIDENCE IS REAL RATHER THAN HYPOTHETICAL: all three explorers hit it on 2026-07-31 while following the project's own guidance, and one routed around it using a scratchpad outside the repo. It cost three agents time on a trap the orchestrator set (defect (7) above).

THE PLANNER'S AMENDMENT PASS PUSHED BACK ON THE ORCHESTRATOR'S EXPECTED INCREMENT PLACEMENT AND WON ON THE DERIVATION, which is the behaviour the brief invited and which should be read as the role separation working rather than as friction. The orchestrator expected the omit behaviour in `inc1`; the planner placed it in `inc2` and argued it two ways. First, THE OMIT IS TRIGGERED BY THE CONTAINMENT PREDICATE AND BY NOTHING CHEAPER, since its scope is exactly the case that survives anchoring and no lexical test separates an explicit `--metrics` at a foreign log from an explicit `--metrics` at the plan's own log spelled differently, which is why explorer A's canonical guard exists at all; putting the omit in `inc1` therefore means putting the PREDICATE in `inc1`, dragging `inc2`'s hardest review question (a NEGATIVE property, established only by adversarial construction) forward into an increment whose question is positive, and leaving `inc2` as a bool turned into a `problems.push`. Second, THE THREE RESPONSES ARE ONLY REVIEWABLE AGAINST EACH OTHER, because the entire content of the decision is that ONE predicate yields DIFFERENT answers on the validator and on the projections, so the defect to catch is a consumer wired to the wrong response, which is invisible unless the three are read side by side. It stated the cost rather than burying it: AFTER `inc1` ALONE THE EXPLICIT-`--metrics` FABRICATION SURVIVES BY DESIGN, so an implementer must not read `inc1`'s checks as closing that defect.

THE PLANNER CAUGHT A TRAP THAT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A FRESH FABRICATION, and it is the most valuable single find of the amendment pass. THE CHEAP IMPLEMENTATION TREATS AN UNSAFE LOG AS AN ABSENT ONE, and `run_next` (`src/main.rs:1200-1205`) turns an absent log into an EMPTY ROUNDS LIST AND STILL PROJECTS, so an `in-progress` step yields `state: awaiting-first-review` and `next: spawn a reviewer for the first review round`. For a project whose real log says CONVERGED that is a NEW false instruction, quieter than the `mark the step complete` one this step exists to kill but still derived from evidence the tool does not have. THE SIDECAR NOW SAYS "UNSAFE IS NOT ABSENT" EXPLICITLY and pins it, with the observation that matters most for the review: A TEST ASSERTING ONLY THE ABSENCE OF `mark the step complete` PASSES AGAINST THIS DEFECT. The planner also established that the change is a REASON and not a SHAPE, since `NextProjection` (`src/next.rs:99-118`) already carries `metrics`, `active_loop`, `resume_state` and `no_active_loop_reason` as `Option`s with `render_human` already rendering the `None` arms, and `status`'s `Projection` matches.

TWO FURTHER HUMAN DECISIONS (2026-07-31). (1) THE `--json` SURFACE GOES THE WIDER WAY: ADD A SERIALISED REASON, so a machine consumer can distinguish an UNSAFE PAIRING from an ABSENT LOG and from an ABSENT PLAN SOURCE, chosen over accepting that `--json` reports only absence and over deferring it to the validation-constraints step. THE PLANNER RAISED THIS RATHER THAN DECIDING IT, having found that `no_active_loop_reason` is `#[serde(skip)]` at `src/next.rs:116` with a doc comment stating "the JSON contract is exactly the fields above", and that `status --json` has no reason field at all, so the OMIT half of the refusal-scope decision is met on both surfaces while the SAY WHY half is met only on the human renderer. THE GROUND FOR GOING WIDE: `--json` is the surface AGENTS actually consume, and this decision exists precisely because an agent ACTS on `next`'s output, so leaving the machine surface silent INVERTS the reasoning that produced the decision. This is a documented-contract change, so the planner was told to specify the field shape and the VALUE VOCABULARY concretely rather than leaving it to the implementer, on the ground that an under-specified vocabulary reproduces the very defect in a new place, and to sweep for sibling "the contract is exactly these fields" claims the change falsifies. A receipt is owed at `q_id` `Q-55-jsonreason`. (2) THE TMPDIR FIX FORK STAYS OPEN with the planner's recommendation standing, rather than being pre-decided; that is a scheduling-level confirmation against an already-decided backlog item, so NO receipt and NO `[[question]]` are owed.

THE PLANNER CORRECTED THE ORCHESTRATOR'S FRAMING A SECOND TIME AND WAS RIGHT AGAIN. The orchestrator offered "the same predicate yielding a THIRD RESPONSE" as its prior for where the `--json` reason belongs; the planner replied that the framing is wrong and the correct one is stronger: THE JSON REASON IS NOT A THIRD RESPONSE, IT IS THE SECOND RENDERING OF ONE RESPONSE. The predicate yields TWO responses (refuse, omit) and the omit has TWO RENDERINGS (human text, JSON). That reframing is what settles the placement into `inc2`: splitting the renderings would have `inc2` COMPUTE a reason, print it to text, and DISCARD it for JSON, with a later increment re-surfacing a value that already existed, a deliberate half-done state bought for nothing; and it would ship a NEW behaviour with its explanation missing on exactly the surface the behaviour exists for, which is strictly worse than the `inc1`-to-`inc2` gap already accepted, since that one leaves an EXISTING defect open for one increment rather than shipping a NEW silence. TWO MECHANICAL REASONS AGREE: the enums are consumed by the code that computes the omission, and `status --json` has NO GOLDEN, so separating would land the unguarded half with no co-located human behaviour to check it against.

THE FIELD VOCABULARY IS SPECIFIED IN THE SIDECAR RATHER THAN LEFT TO THE IMPLEMENTER, on the orchestrator's instruction that an under-specified vocabulary would reproduce the very defect in a new place. Closed kebab-case enums following the in-file `LoopState` precedent (`src/next.rs:187-189`), always present and `null` in the normal case since neither file uses `skip_serializing_if`: `metrics_absent_reason` on BOTH projections (`log-absent` / `log-not-this-project`); `no_active_loop_reason` RETYPED from `Option<String>` and UN-SKIPPED (`no-plan-steps` / `all-steps-complete` / `no-ready-step` / `metrics-not-this-project`), where the first three map back to the exact strings printed today so the retype is behaviour-preserving on the human surface and its golden, and retyping beats a parallel field because one reason with one type and two renderings cannot disagree with itself; and `resume_state_absent_reason` (`ledger-absent` / `no-resume-section` / `ledger-not-this-project`), included because without it the identical defect lands on a third field while `src/main.rs:1208-1212` already distinguishes the two existing causes in separate branches that both collapse to `None`. A CORRELATION RULE lets a consumer JOIN rather than guess: an unsafe pairing sets `metrics_absent_reason: log-not-this-project` AND `no_active_loop_reason: metrics-not-this-project` in one output, the shared `not-this-project` token being deliberate. TWO EXCLUSIONS ARE STATED AS DECISIONS rather than omissions: no reason beside `status`'s `plan` (single cause, informs nobody), and no malformed-log variant (no such state is distinguished today, so it would be new behaviour rather than a reason for existing behaviour).

THE SIBLING SWEEP FOUND TWO MORE FALSIFIED DOC CLAIMS BEYOND THE ONE THE ORCHESTRATOR NAMED, AND THEY ARE TIED. `src/next.rs:95-97` and `src/main.rs:560-563` both ENUMERATE the causes of an absent part and are now SHORT BY ONE, and the enumeration is exactly what this change makes reportable, so leaving it two-thirds complete would be the defect in miniature; they are TIED because `src/next.rs:96` says it "mirrors `status`'s `Projection`", so fixing one and not the other would leave a claim of CORRESPONDENCE asserting away a real difference. All three change together. ONE PRE-EXISTING MISMATCH IS FLAGGED AND NOT FIXED BY ADDING BEHAVIOUR: `src/next.rs:108-109` says `active_loop` is `None` when "every pending step blocked", a distinction `no_loop_reason` (`src/next.rs:953-961`) does not draw, folding that case into its third string; the enum's variants must match what the code DISTINGUISHES, so the COMMENT is what is wrong. THE NEGATIVES ARE RECORDED TOO, which is the part that makes the sweep evidence rather than anecdote: `#[serde(skip)]` appears EXACTLY ONCE in the whole of `src/`, so there is no second silently-dropped field anywhere; no `skip_serializing_if` in either file; no other doc comment claims its JSON output is exhaustive.

AN ASYMMETRY THAT DECIDES HOW THE ACCEPTANCE CHECKS MUST BE WRITTEN: `next`'s JSON is pinned by a BYTE-COMPARE GOLDEN (`GOLDEN_JSON`, `src/next.rs:1705`, asserted at `:1762`), so adding a field BREAKS THAT TEST and the contract change cannot land silently, with the golden diff as the reviewable artifact; but `status --json` has NO GOLDEN AND NO SERIALISATION TEST AT ALL (`src/main.rs:1104` is its only path), so that half is UNGUARDED and a mistake there fails nothing. That is why the sidecar's checks for the `status --json` half RUN THE BINARY rather than relying on the suite.

A THIRD BACKLOG STEP WAS SCHEDULED BY THE HUMAN (2026-07-31) from an item the planner raised rather than decided: `--json` IS SILENTLY IGNORED ALONGSIDE `--resume`, because `run_status` returns from `run_resume` at `src/main.rs:1067-1069` BEFORE any serialisation, so `status --resume --json` produces human-readable output and the flag has no effect. Scheduling decision against the standing backlog, so NO `[[question]]` and NO receipt are owed. IT IS AN INCONSISTENCY RATHER THAN A GAP, which is why it is worth scheduling at all: this project ALREADY treats a silently-ignored flag as deserving a clap constraint, with `--ledger-fragment` carrying `requires = "resume"` at `src/main.rs:465` for that exact stated reason, "the flag is meaningless without it, and would otherwise be silently ignored on an exit-0 run", and the same sentence recurring on `--workflow-spec` (`:441-443`) and shorter on `render --strict` (`:524-526`). CORRECTION TO THIS LEDGER'S FIRST STATEMENT OF THAT PRECEDENT, made by the PLANNER against its own earlier framing and VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY BY THE ORCHESTRATOR, which had already propagated the wrong version here and to the human: THE RELATION IS `conflicts_with`, NOT `requires`. `--resume` and `--json` are two MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE OUTPUT MODES, so the governing precedent is `audit --out` at `src/main.rs:556-558` ("Conflicts with --json, which writes no file"), and a `requires` IN EITHER DIRECTION WOULD BREAK `status --json` STANDING ALONE, which is a documented surface with a README example; the orchestrator confirmed by running it that `status --source <plan> --json` emits real JSON today. THE STEP PINS BOTH DIRECTIONS in its acceptance check for the reason the planner gave, that A BACKWARDS FIX PASSES THE DEFECT CHECK AND FAILS ONLY THE CONTROL. THE SEVERITY IS DELIBERATELY UNDERSTATED IN THE STEP so a backlog item does not distort the queue: this is a FALSE SILENCE, not a false assertion, since the caller gets output that is visibly not JSON and it fails at the consumer immediately rather than propagating a wrong value, which makes it MILDER than the defects the enforcement-tier step fixes.

A PLANNER PROCESS SLIP, DISCLOSED BY THE PLANNER ITSELF AND VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY BY THE ORCHESTRATOR: one of its bash calls ran from the MAIN repository instead of its worktree, because agent-thread working directory resets between calls and its explicit `cd` prefix had slipped, and it executed `render docs/plans/agent-scaffold.plan.toml` against MAIN. NO DAMAGE: main's generated view was already current so the render was a no-op, and `git status --short` on main is empty, confirmed by the orchestrator at `4c57028` rather than taken on the planner's word. THE LESSON IS SYMMETRIC WITH DEFECT (8) BELOW, which was the orchestrator making the same error in the opposite direction: NEVER RELY ON AN INHERITED WORKING DIRECTORY, in either role, always pass the path explicitly.

RECEIPT PLACEMENT FOR THIS FOLD, DECIDED DELIBERATELY AFTER DEFECT (8) BELOW RATHER THAN DRIFTING INTO IT: the `Q-55-refusalscope` receipt already sits on the BRANCH, and the `Q-55-jsonreason` receipt will be appended THERE TOO once the planner's worktree is idle, so that ALL of this fold's receipts stay CONTIGUOUS and reach main together at merge. Splitting them across main and the branch would guarantee a conflict in an append-only JSONL at the next rebase, and duplicating one onto main would leave TWO copies of a single receipt after the merge. THE CONSEQUENCE TO EXPECT, so it is not misread as a fault: until the branch merges, MAIN'S LOG RUNS BEHIND THE BRANCH'S and this ledger cites receipts main does not yet hold. The receipt is NOT written while the planner is mid-flight in that worktree, since concurrent writes to one worktree are the same class of problem as defects (5) and (8).

ORCHESTRATOR DEFECT (8), AND IT IS THE SAME ROOT CAUSE AS DEFECT (5) IN A NEW DISGUISE: A PERSISTED SHELL `cd` PUT ORCHESTRATOR WORK ON THE BRANCH INSTEAD OF MAIN. Verifying the planner's `README.md:226` citation required reading a file in the worktree, and the command began `cd .claude/worktrees/plan-q55`; the shell working directory PERSISTS BETWEEN CALLS, so the next three commands ran there too. CONSEQUENCES, all verified rather than assumed: the `Q-55-refusalscope` receipt was appended to THE BRANCH's `docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl` rather than main's, so main stands at 237 records and the branch at 238; the commit `7400f12` was made ON THE BRANCH and contains ONLY that one-line receipt, while its message describes the planner pass, the refusal-scope decision and the TMPDIR scheduling, none of which are in it; and the ledger edit itself, made through the Edit tool with an ABSOLUTE path, correctly landed on main but was left UNCOMMITTED. The `render --check` and the ASCII grep reported alongside that commit ran in the WORKTREE, so the ASCII check was VACUOUS, it read the branch's unmodified ledger and not the file that had just been edited; re-run against the real file it returns 0, but it was reported before it had been earned. THE ORCHESTRATOR ALSO REPORTED THIS TO THE HUMAN AS "recorded on main at `7400f12` ... 238 records valid", WHICH WAS FALSE ON BOTH COUNTS, and this is the second time in this task that the orchestrator has stated a check result to the human without confirming the check measured what it claimed (defect (4) was the grep whose zero result was evidence about the pattern). NO HISTORY SURGERY WAS DONE: the receipt is legitimately in the metrics log and reaches main when the branch merges, duplicating it onto main now would produce TWO copies of one receipt after the merge, and this project's practice is to record a past error rather than rewrite it. SO NOTE FOR THE PLAN REVIEWERS OF THIS BRANCH, so it is not raised as a finding: `7400f12`'s message OVERSTATES ITS CONTENT and this paragraph is the correction; the ledger narrative, not that message, is authoritative. NOTE ALSO that until the branch merges, this ledger cites "receipt 238" while main's own log holds 237, which is a KNOWN transient and not a validation failure. THE CURE, which generalises defect (5)'s absolute-paths-to-`git worktree add` rule rather than repeating it: NEVER let a `cd` into a worktree persist. Read worktree files with an absolute path or `git -C`, and run every orchestrator write with an explicit `git -C <main repo>` or from a command that begins by returning to the main repository root.

THE PLANNER SELF-CORRECTED ON THE CLAP RELATION FOR THE `status-resume-ignores-json` STEP, AND THE ORCHESTRATOR HAD ALREADY PROPAGATED THE WRONG VERSION into this ledger and to the human before the correction arrived; the corrected text is folded into the backlog-step paragraph above. THE ORCHESTRATOR VERIFIED THE CORRECTION ITSELF rather than accepting it: `src/main.rs:464-466` does carry `requires = "resume"` on `--ledger-fragment` with the quoted reason, `src/main.rs:556-558` does carry `conflicts_with = "json"` on `audit --out`, and `status --source <plan> --json` DOES emit real JSON today, which is the control that proves a `requires` in either direction would break a documented surface. THE STEP IS `status-resume-ignores-json`, order 96, `not-started`, one increment at `low_risk`, `blocked_by = []`. The planner argued the class rather than defaulting it and named the condition that voids it: the classification is scoped to fork (A), the clap constraint, and IF FORK (B) IS CHOSEN THE CLASSIFICATION DOES NOT HOLD AND THE STEP IS RE-CLASSIFIED BEFORE BUILDING, NOT WAIVED AFTER, which is the correct shape given this task's recorded history of reclassifying after the fact to clear a bar. It also recorded a dependency it deliberately did NOT encode in `blocked_by`: fork (B) must not be built before `workflow-enforcement-tier` inc2, which defines `resume_state_absent_reason` with exactly the three causes a resume JSON surface would report, so building (B) first would mint a SECOND VOCABULARY for one set of causes; encoding that would make the step falsely blocked and drag it into this step's wake, defeating the point of scheduling it independently.

PLAN REVIEW ROUND 1 IS OPEN (2026-07-31) ON THE `workflow-enforcement-tier` FOLD, branch `plan/q55-enforcement` at `6df032c` (rebased onto main first). THE ARTIFACT IS CLASSIFIED `risky` AT LOOP-OPEN, so TWO consecutive clean rounds are required, and the classification is argued rather than inherited: this fold is far larger than step 90's (three increments plus two new backlog steps plus a heavily rewritten sidecar), it SPECIFIES A FIELD VOCABULARY AN IMPLEMENTER WILL FOLLOW LITERALLY so a defect there propagates into three `risky` code increments, and the calibration data says a COVERAGE CLAIM ABOUT AN ENFORCEMENT BOUNDARY is this project's worst-behaved artifact species (step 92: six rounds, fifteen findings, all prose, against a project median of two). THE ARTIFACT IS FROZEN AT `6df032c` FOR THIS ROUND, which is the explicit cure for orchestrator defect (1), the risk-class drift where a `low_risk` artifact was allowed to grow after classification.

TWO WORKTREE-ISOLATED REVIEWERS ON DIFFERENT MODELS, both created from the BRANCH TIP rather than main (defect (2)'s cure) with ABSOLUTE paths (defect (5)'s cure) and briefed to keep `TMPDIR` OUTSIDE any git repository (defect (7)'s cure). LENS A, EXECUTABILITY, `claude-opus-5`: read the sidecar as INSTRUCTIONS TO FOLLOW rather than as a document, simulate each increment, run the acceptance checks that can run today and confirm the pre-fix red cases ARE red, test whether the field vocabulary can actually distinguish every case it must, and look for instructions that would damage the repo if followed literally. THIS LENS RUNS AT ROUND 1 DELIBERATELY, discharging the step-90 lesson recorded as "run an executability lens EARLY, not at round 4", which was learned when four consecutive rounds read a sidecar as a DOCUMENT and it looked fine. LENS B, FIDELITY AND GROUNDING, `claude-sonnet-5`: mechanical and exhaustive, checking every `file:line` citation by opening it, every quotation by diffing it, every behavioural claim by running it, every count and closed enumeration by an independent command, the sidecars against the exploration records including whether measurements are reported WITH their stated caveats, internal consistency across the three sidecars and the TOML, and that every cited receipt exists with a matching `chosen`. Both were given the decided-and-not-to-be-relitigated list, and both were told the distinction that keeps that list from suppressing real findings: a place where the sidecar MISREPRESENTS a decision, contradicts itself about one, or would not produce the decided behaviour IS in scope.

THE FINDINGS FILES CARRY A PHASE SLOT THE SHIPPED CONVENTION DOES NOT HAVE, applied by hand: `workflow-enforcement-tier-planreview-r1-reviewer-executability.md` and `-fidelity.md` under `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.reviews/`. The convention in `AGENTS.md` is `<step>-<role>-<disambiguator>.md` with NO phase slot, and the calibration analysis established that this is what produced step 93's five-triage-file namespace collision between its plan-review and work-review loops sharing one step slug. `workflow-enforcement-tier` WILL have a work review under this same slug, so the collision is not hypothetical here; inserting `planreview-r1` now preempts it without waiting for the enforcement fix, which is scheduled among the three deterministic instrumentation checks.

PLAN REVIEW ROUND 1 RESULT (record 238): 15 FINDINGS, 14 VALID AND 1 VALID-BUT-ACCEPT-RESIDUAL, ZERO DISMISSED. Adjusted severities 0 critical, 1 high, 4 medium, 10 low; outcome `new_valid`, streak 0 OF 2. NO HIGH OR CRITICAL WAS DISMISSED SO NO BACKSTOP RE-CHECK IS OWED. The triager changed three severities, in both directions: EX-4 UPGRADED medium -> high, EX-6 and F-1 DOWNGRADED medium -> low.

THE HIGH IS A DESTRUCTIVE INSTRUCTION, AND THE TRIAGER'S REPRODUCTION IMPROVED ON THE REVIEWER'S. `EX-4`: acceptance check 7 names the EXACT PATH of this repository's LIVE, TRACKED `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.ledger.md` (790 lines, `## RESUME STATE` at `:329`) and tells the implementer to write a DECOY there from the repo root. The reviewer found the instruction; the TRIAGER RAN THE CHECK WITHOUT THE DECOY and it STILL printed this repository's RESUME STATE verbatim, which establishes something stronger than the reviewer claimed: THE REAL LEDGER ALREADY IS THE LEAK TARGET, so the decoy is not merely dangerous but UNNECESSARY AND SELF-DEFEATING. The fix is DELETION-ONLY and single-site, one clause at line 312, and the triager verified the check still works after it.

EX-1, THE ROUND'S STRUCTURAL CLAIM, IS VALID BUT ITS FRAMING DOES NOT SURVIVE, WHICH IS EXACTLY WHY THE TRIAGER WAS TOLD TO ATTACK IT. The reviewer claimed the sidecar's requirement is UNBUILDABLE, every escape blocked by another clause. ALL ITS CODE FACTS REPRODUCE (`render_human` is a pure function of `NextProjection`, which carries no path fields; `status` can name them inline at `src/main.rs:1125-1128` and `next` cannot), BUT THE TRIAGER FOUND THREE ESCAPES THE REVIEWER MISSED: a `#[serde(skip)]` detail field carrying the paths, which the cited blocking clause does not reach because it rejects a parallel SERIALISED field and which contributes nothing to the `GOLDEN_JSON` diff; a HAND-WRITTEN `Serialize` emitting the bare kebab token, which defeats the reviewer's "serde would serialise it as an object" block, that being true only of a DERIVED impl; and changing `render_human`'s signature, which nothing in the sidecar constrains and which the finding ITSELF proposes as its own remedy. SO THE SEVERITY HELD AT `medium` RATHER THAN RISING. WHAT SURVIVES IS STILL REAL AND STILL MATTERS: the under-specification means THE BARE TOKEN PASSES EVERY RUNNABLE CHECK AND WOULD SHIP. This is the second time this task's triage layer has corrected a finding's reasoning while upholding its substance, and it is the strongest available argument for keeping the separate-triager rule.

THE TWO LENSES RETURNED DISJOINT SETS, WITH NOTHING RAISED BY BOTH. That is the intended consequence of region-scoped lenses rather than a fault, but it must be read honestly: NO FINDING IN THIS ROUND HAS CROSS-LENS CORROBORATION, so each rests on one reviewer plus the triager's reproduction. EX-3 AND EX-7 ARE ONE ROOT CAUSE SEEN TWICE and must be fixed together.

THE FIX SHAPE IS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED, WHICH IS THE POINT OF ASKING FOR IT: SEVEN OF THE FOURTEEN ADMIT A DELETION-ONLY OR NUMBER-EDIT-ONLY FIX, and only EX-1, EX-2 and EX-10 need new prose, ONE CLAUSE EACH. Given this task's five retrospective and one prospective confirmations that prose-authoring passes manufacture the next round's finding while deletion-class passes re-seed nothing, that ratio is the best fix-pass profile this task has had. ONE CORRECTION TO A REVIEWER'S OWN SINGLE-SITE CLAIM: F-1 IS NOT SINGLE-SITE, the false "four" appears THREE times and not the one flagged. All sidecar edits need a re-render, since `agent-scaffold.md` carries the same citations.

THE ACCEPTED RESIDUAL IS `F-5`, THE DANGLING `validation-constraints` HANDLE, and the triager's ground is the right one: it is PRE-EXISTING DEBT rather than a defect of this fold (0 references in `plan.toml` on main; the step was human-decided 2026-07-30 and NEVER ENTERED as a plan item), so THE RIGHT ACTION IS ENTERING THAT STEP AS ITS OWN PLAN ITEM, NOT PATCHING THIS FOLD to stop citing it. Do not re-raise it here. Note the fold cites the handle in BOTH the sidecar and the TOML's own `Q-55` text, so the citation is load-bearing for the queue and should not simply be deleted.

TWO CALIBRATION ITEMS FROM THIS ROUND, BOTH ABOUT REVIEWERS OVERSTATING THEMSELVES. (1) `F-5`'S OWN COUNT IS WRONG: it says 90 step slugs where there are 95, though its load-bearing claim (no `validation-constraints` slug) is correct. (2) MORE SERIOUS, THE TRIAGER FOUND AN ITEM BOTH REVIEWERS MISSED: `test-tmpdir-repo-assumption.md:76` calls step 93 `deferred` when it is `complete` (`plan.toml:1279`), a one-word fix, AND THE FIDELITY LENS NAMED ORDER 93 AMONG THE CROSS-REFERENCES IT CHECKED. That makes this round's ONE DEMONSTRATED GAP IN A STATED COVERAGE CLAIM, which is the precise failure mode the region-scoped-lens discipline exists to prevent and which therefore did NOT get prevented here. Record it against the discipline rather than against the reviewer: a lens can name a region and still not sweep it, so a coverage CLAIM is not coverage EVIDENCE, and a future brief should ask for the enumeration itself rather than an assertion that one was done.

THE ROUND 1 FIX PASS LANDED AT `8756578` AND IS THE BEST-SHAPED FIX PASS THIS TASK HAS PRODUCED: NET +2 LINES across three authored sidecars plus the regenerated view. THE MEASURED SHAPE, which the planner was asked to count accurately because the orchestrator uses it directly: FIFTEEN EDITS ACROSS FOURTEEN FINDINGS, of which TEN were DELETION-ONLY OR NUMBER-ONLY, THREE were NARROWINGS (replacing a false clause with a shorter true one), THREE were NEW PROSE at one clause or sentence each, and TWO were CORRECTIONS (replacing a false statement with a corrected one of similar length, adding no claim). THE THREE PROSE ADDITIONS ARE EXACTLY THE THREE THE TRIAGER PREDICTED, EX-1, EX-2 AND EX-10, AND NO MORE. THE ESCAPE VALVE WAS NOT USED, and the planner reported that nothing required widening, which is a different and better statement than "the valve was not needed because nothing was hard".

FOUR FIX-PASS JUDGEMENT CALLS WORTH KEEPING. (1) EX-2 WAS FIXED AS THE TRIAGER DESCRIBED IT AND NOT AS THE REVIEWER DID, which is the instruction working: the two metrics variants were ALREADY DISJOINT, and the real defect was line 217's existence qualifier colliding with the correlation rule for an explicit `--metrics` outside the root whose leaf does not exist; deleting the qualifier makes the variants ORDERED, which one precedence sentence then resolves, and the same sentence closes a second sub-claim. (2) EX-1 WAS FIXED AS UNDER-SPECIFICATION RATHER THAN AS BUILDABILITY, one clause stating the enum is the machine value and the caller assembles the human message from paths it already holds, with NO paragraph about the escapes; the planner deliberately did NOT touch acceptance check 14h and gave the reason, that under this carrier no new serialised fields appear so the check's statement stays true. (3) EX-9 TOOK THE BETTER FORM OVER THE BARE DELETION, since striking only the false clause would have left a citation to a test file with nothing marking it as one. (4) THE TRIAGER'S EXTRA ITEM TOOK THE DELETION OPTION RATHER THAN THE WORD SWAP: replacing `deferred` with `complete` would have left a phrase reading wrong for a finished step AND would drift again on any future status change, so dropping the label removes both the false claim and the recurrence, at no cost to the bullet's point. THAT IS THE DELETION-PREFERRED RULE APPLIED WITH JUDGEMENT RATHER THAN MECHANICALLY, and it is the shape to repeat.

PLAN REVIEW ROUND 2 IS OPEN (2026-07-31), artifact FROZEN at `8756578`, streak 0 OF 2 so a clean round takes it to 1. TWO WORKTREE-ISOLATED REVIEWERS ON DIFFERENT MODELS, both from the FIX COMMIT. LENS A, FIX-INDUCED RESIDUE, `claude-sonnet-5`: verify each of the 14 fixes individually landed, hunt for a fix that is locally correct but contradicts nearby text, grep for anything still depending on what was removed, and CHECK FOR OVERREACH, since an unrequested change is a finding even if it looks like an improvement, because it was not reviewed. Its highest-priority region is the three prose additions and three narrowings, because that is precisely where this task's measured pattern predicts the next defect. LENS B, INC2 BUILDABILITY, `claude-opus-5`: round 1's executability lens answered a QUALIFIED NO on inc2 and the fix added ONE CLAUSE for each blocking finding, so this lens exists to test whether that actually closed it, briefed to be SCEPTICAL on the ground that a one-clause fix to a structural under-specification is exactly the kind of thing that looks resolved and is not. It judges the fix against the TRIAGER'S statement of the problem (a bare token passes every runnable check and would ship) rather than the reviewer's refuted framing, and it must enumerate input cases hunting for one that is UNREPRESENTABLE, a variant that is UNREACHABLE, and two causes that COLLIDE, since round 1 found one of each class.

A NEW BRIEFING DISCIPLINE IS IN FORCE FROM THIS ROUND, discharging round 1's own lesson: BOTH REVIEWERS ARE REQUIRED TO REPORT THEIR SWEEP AS AN ENUMERATION RATHER THAN AS AN ASSERTION, listing which files, sections, fixes or input cases they actually covered and saying explicitly what they did not. The lesson it discharges is that round 1's fidelity lens NAMED order 93 among the cross-references it checked and still missed a defect inside it, so A COVERAGE CLAIM IS NOT COVERAGE EVIDENCE. Both were also told that a clean result is a perfectly good outcome and not to manufacture findings to look thorough, which is the counterweight to briefing them adversarially.

PLAN REVIEW ROUND 2 RESULT (record 239): 11 FINDINGS, 10 VALID AND 1 ACCEPT-RESIDUAL, ZERO DISMISSED, at 0 critical, 0 high, 3 medium, 8 low. Outcome `new_valid`, STREAK STILL 0 OF 2, and the loop is now on ROUND 3 OF A CAP OF 5. Two severities were DOWNGRADED (`INC2-1` and `INC2-5`, medium -> low) and none upgraded. The accepted residual is `INC2-7`. AN ID COLLISION WAS RESOLVED IN TRIAGE: both reviewers independently used `R2-1` for DIFFERENT findings, which is ORCHESTRATOR DEFECT (9), assigning distinct FILENAMES but not distinct FINDING-ID NAMESPACES; the triager renamed them to `RES-1` and `INC2-1` through `INC2-10` with a mapping table, and the round-3 briefs will assign id prefixes up front.

THE TWO LENSES CONTRADICTED EACH OTHER AND THE TRIAGER RULED, WHICH IS THE SINGLE MOST USEFUL THING THIS ROUND PRODUCED. The residue lens concluded there was NO fix-induced prose residue, having named the three one-clause prose additions as the region it cleared. The inc2 lens's `INC2-2` said the target text round 1's EX-3 fix WROTE is falsified by inc2's own third cause of `active_loop: None`. THE TRIAGER RULED THE RESIDUE LENS WRONG AND `INC2-2` UPHELD, on evidence stronger than the reviewer's own case: the `8756578` diff shows the fix changed the doc-impact instruction from "reconcile the comment TO WHAT THE CODE DISTINGUISHES" to "reconcile the comment TO THE TARGET TEXT GIVEN ABOVE", in the same commit that added a TWO-CAUSE target text, while inc2 adds a THIRD cause and check 14e pins it literally. SO EX-3's FIX DID NOT MERELY LEAVE A DEFECT, IT CONVERTED A CORRECT-BY-DEFERRAL INSTRUCTION INTO AN INCORRECT-BY-SPECIFICATION ONE. That is fix-induced residue of the classic shape, and it lands squarely inside the region the residue lens declared clear.

THE DIAGNOSIS OF WHY BOTH COVERAGE MISSES HAPPENED IS THE LESSON WORTH KEEPING, AND IT IS SHARPER THAN "SWEEP MORE". The residue lens tested EX-3's sentence THE WAY YOU TEST A DESCRIPTIVE CLAIM, opening the cited code and confirming the statement is true OF THE TREE, which it is; the claim it needed to test was about what THE INCREMENT WILL PRODUCE. Round 1's fidelity lens missed order 93 the same way. SO BOTH DEMONSTRATED COVERAGE GAPS IN THIS REVIEW SIT WHERE THE TEST APPLIED WAS WRONG FOR THE CLAIM'S TENSE, not where the sweep was too narrow. A PLAN is a set of claims about a FUTURE state, so a reviewer that verifies each sentence against the CURRENT tree will systematically pass exactly the sentences a plan exists to get right. Independently damning: the residue lens's own enumeration of what it cleared LISTED the three prose additions as EX-1, EX-2 and EX-10 and OMITTED EX-3's target-text sentence, which the diff shows is a prose addition of exactly that class, so the enumeration discipline caught its own gap the moment someone read it against the diff. THE ENUMERATION DISCIPLINE THEREFORE EARNED ITS KEEP ON ITS FIRST OUTING, not by preventing the miss but by making it VISIBLE AND ATTRIBUTABLE.

EX-1's FIX DID NOT FAIL, AND THE TRIAGER REFUTED THE LENS'S HEADLINE WHILE KEEPING A NARROWER DEFECT. The inc2 lens's QUALIFIED NO rested on the carrier clause not reaching `next`'s human surface. THE STRUCTURAL HALF REPRODUCES, HARDER THAN CLAIMED: the triager scanned every print statement in `run_next` and found exactly two, at `src/main.rs:1237` and `:1239`, both inside the `if args.json` branch, so `next`'s entire human output IS `render_human`'s return value. BUT THE CLAUSE IS A DIRECTIVE AND THE REVIEWER GRADED IT AS A DESCRIPTION: nothing about an unsafe-pairing message exists today, so "the caller assembles that message" is not true of `run_status` EITHER as a statement about current code, and `run_next` does hold both paths (`args.metrics` at `:1200`, the source at `:1180-1184`). The fix wrote what round 1's triage specified and the target property is achievable on both commands. WHAT SURVIVES IS A CHECK GAP RATHER THAN A SPECIFICATION GAP: the path-naming requirement is PINNED BY NO CHECK, since 14b asks only for "a reason naming the unsafe pairing", and the fix is a PHRASE SUBSTITUTION INSIDE CHECK 14b, not a third pass of prose over line 212. THIS IS THE SECOND ROUND RUNNING IN WHICH THE TRIAGE LAYER HAS REFUTED A REVIEWER'S STRONG FRAMING WHILE UPHOLDING A NARROWER SUBSTANCE, and it is the strongest evidence this task has produced for the separate-triager rule.

INC2 IS BUILDABLE AFTER ALL, ON DIFFERENT GROUNDS THAN EITHER REVIEWER GAVE: the triager's answer is QUALIFIED YES, and THE REAL BLOCKER IS `INC2-4`, NOT `INC2-1`. It MEASURED that an implementer who builds the fixture per check 2 and applies check 14d's one stated precondition gets `state: awaiting-first-review`, which is the output 14d FORBIDS as the post-fix answer, rather than check 14b's stated red, which needs a borrowed slug the check never mentions; and the file NAMES THAT FIXTURE THREE WAYS (`$SCRATCH`, an undefined `$FIXTURE`, and a `p.plan.toml` no fixture produces). Everything else is verification coverage rather than executability, two specified rules stated and unpinned, both fixable inside checks that already exist. WITH `INC2-2`, `INC2-3` AND `INC2-4` TAKEN, INC2 SHOULD BE EXECUTABLE FROM THE SIDECAR ALONE.

THE FIX PROFILE HELD A SECOND TIME: SEVEN OF TEN admit a deletion-only or non-prose fix and only `INC2-5`, `INC2-9` and `INC2-10` need one new clause each. EVERY SITE COUNT WAS GREPPED OVER ALL THREE SIDECARS AND THE PLAN TOML, discharging `RES-1`'s own lesson, which was that round 1's F-4 fix grepped only "the steps directory" and so left an identical sentence in the TOML at the old line number; only `RES-1` has a site outside the primary sidecar. TWO MINOR ERRORS INSIDE THE FINDINGS FILES, neither load-bearing: the inc2 lens cites line 302 for a sentence at 303, and three of its transcripts are abridged without marking.

NO ROUND RECORD IS OWED FOR THIS PASS. A design pass produces exploration records, not review rounds, so nothing is appended to `docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl` and no convergence counter moves; the artifact that will carry round records is the plan fold that follows.

BOOKKEEPING CALL MADE AT DISPATCH, AND IT IS A THIRD LIVE INSTANCE FOR `Q-69`. The pre-compaction anchor instructed the orchestrator to "register the metrics-path mechanism as an `exploring` item", but the generated `ISOLATION_POLICY_FRAGMENT` (`src/isolation_policy.rs:33`) enumerates the orchestrator's direct-on-main edits as exactly four, flipping a step's status, declaring an increment, recording a round record, and moving the ledger's resume anchor, and authoring a `[[question]]` is NOT among them. That is exactly the contradiction `Q-69` holds open and `Q-69` is deferred past the rename, so NO new `[[question]]` was authored. The design pass runs as a DESIGN SUB-QUESTION UNDER THE ALREADY-DECIDED `Q-55`, with this ledger paragraph as the durable record, and the SECOND PLANNER PASS registers whatever the plan needs, which costs nothing because that pass is already scheduled and is rewriting the fold anyway. The cost is that the item is not visible in the plan's human-facing Open Questions queue while the pass runs. The precedent is the step-90 sidecar's `Q-52` sub-decisions and the `Q-65` resequencing, both recorded as sub-decisions under an already-decided question with no new `[[question]]` and no receipt owed.
