NOTE FOR THE `Q-69` DESIGN PASS WHEN IT RUNS: this is now the THIRD recorded case of the orchestrator needing an edit outside the closed four (waiver authoring twice, question registration once), which is evidence about the LIST rather than about the orchestrator, and it should be weighed alongside the already-owed planner item to update that list.

PLANNER WORK OWED, NONE STARTED, CARRIED FORWARD: the new step closing `X4`'s code half plus `X6` (canonicalise the prune's temp-dir gate, reject a relative temp dir, needs its OWN risk class since it changes what the prune deletes); the `[[question]]` against the `mutation` step reconciling it with `Q-66`; the step-92 sidecar's `src/manifest.rs` mis-citation; the step-90 sidecar's stale "no drift guard" claim; UPDATING THE ORCHESTRATOR'S CLOSED DIRECT-EDIT LIST to cover waiver authoring, which has now been needed twice; and the STALE PROSE STATUS NARRATIVE described in its own paragraph below. UNROUTED PACK CONTRADICTION: `.agents/prompts/implementer.md` tells the implementer to set `[[step]].status` and re-render while the generated isolation-policy fragment lists that as an ORCHESTRATOR direct-on-main edit, worked around so far by instructing each implementer explicitly.

STANDING DISCIPLINES LEARNED THE HARD WAY THIS SESSION, which every future agent brief on this task should carry: (a) REVIEWER MODEL DIVERSITY is now honoured and must stay so, since the first mixed round immediately produced a split (a `claude-sonnet-5` lens returned zero while a `claude-opus-5` lens returned three valid findings) and any "two lenses independently agreed" recorded BEFORE 2026-07-31 rests on same-model agreement and is weaker than it was presented as; (b) SCOPE A REVIEW LENS TO A REGION, not to a list of sentences, because the one lens that returned a wrong zero had both its misses INSIDE its remit but OUTSIDE its sweep; (c) BRIEF EVERY AGENT TO USE A SCRATCH `TMPDIR` and report its `/tmp` count, after one reviewer left 32272 directories there; (d) PASS ABSOLUTE PATHS TO `git worktree add`, never relative, because a persisted shell `cd` silently nested two reviewer worktrees inside the implementer's; (e) PREFER DELETION TO AUTHORED PROSE in any fix pass, now with five retrospective and one PROSPECTIVE confirmation.

THE CALIBRATION ANALYSIS IS DONE (2026-07-31), AT `docs/plans/workflow-calibration.explorations/calibration-analysis.md`. A SEPARATE ANALYST THAT DID NONE OF THE GATHERING answered the six pre-registered questions, RECOMMENDED KEEPING ALL THREE CONSTANTS UNCHANGED, and CORRECTED FIVE CLAIMS IN THIS LEDGER, four of them the orchestrator's. Its headline is that THE CONSTANTS ARE FINE AND THE INSTRUMENTATION IS NOT.

Q1, THE BLOCKING QUESTION, ANSWERED WITH A MEASUREMENT NOBODY HAD EXTRACTED. The analyst isolated every within-episode transition where round K was `clean` at `consecutive_clean == 1` and a round K+1 nevertheless ran; that is EXACTLY the round a bar of 1 would have skipped, and crucially IT IS NOT CENSORED, because the bar of 2 forces it to happen. There are 22, all on `risky` artifacts. THE ENFORCED SECOND ROUND RETURNED `new_valid` 3 of 22 times (13.6 percent, Wilson 4.7 to 33.3) AND FOUND SOMETHING MEDIUM-OR-WORSE 0 OF 22 TIMES (0.0 percent, upper bound 14.9). The complete severity inventory of everything all 22 ever found is THREE LOW FINDINGS; nineteen found nothing at all. SO ONE VERIFICATION ROUND IS SUFFICIENT AT THE SEVERITY LEVEL THAT MATTERS, with a residual-risk ceiling of 14.9 percent on medium-or-worse. CORRECTION TO THIS LEDGER: the 3-of-21-unchanged-rounds figure and this 3-of-22 figure ARE THE SAME THREE EVENTS, so the corpus holds ONE measurement of the second round's value, not two independent ones, and the orchestrator's framing implied otherwise.

THE COUNTER-EVIDENCE ON STEP 93'S OWN ARTIFACT, WHICH THE ANALYST RAISED AGAINST ITS OWN RECOMMENDATION. Round 4's triage recorded `RG1`/`MU1`, a MEDIUM test defect, as `introduced_by_prior_fix` blamed on round 3's `AD1a` fix. And 20 of the 22 recorded injections corpus-wide are `class = prose` (90.9 percent, 72.2 to 97.5), while STEP 93'S ONE AUTHORED ELEMENT IS A PROSE NARROWING CLAUSE. THE ANALYST'S PRESCRIPTION: scope the verification lens to that clause's region. AND A CONDITIONAL WORTH ACTING ON: "if step 93's fix set were cut to the two pure deletions only, I would raise that to HIGH" confidence, which makes dropping the `RG4` clause to an accepted residual a live and evidence-backed option rather than a shortcut.

Q2 AND Q3, AND A DIRECT CONTRADICTION OF THIS LEDGER. Keep the `risky` bar at 2 (MEDIUM, the analyst's closest call): it costs 1.09 extra rounds per risky artifact, about 8 rounds per additional finding and INFINITE rounds per additional medium-or-worse, but the ceiling is real and one medium would settle it permanently. KEEP THE CAP AT 5 (MEDIUM), AND THIS CONTRADICTS THE ORCHESTRATOR'S EARLIER CLAIM THAT THE CAP IS INCONSISTENT WITH THE BAR. A fitted two-state chain (calibrating at 23.4 percent against 22.7 observed) gives escalation-at-cap-5 of 23.4 percent under bar 2 but 2.9 PERCENT UNDER BAR 1: an EIGHTFOLD difference driven by the bar, not the cap. THE CAP IS NOT THE BINDING CONSTRAINT, THE BAR IS. Both loops that actually ran a sixth round STILL FAILED at cap 6, so raising the cap buys little. AND THE REFRAME THAT MATTERS: all 5 escalation records carry `human_decision: "decision"` and NONE is a bare resume, which answers the step brief's own useful-versus-friction question at 5 of 5 useful. ESCALATION IS THE PRODUCT, NOT THE FAILURE MODE.

Q4 IS UNANSWERABLE AT HIGH CONFIDENCE, Q5 SAYS THE EXTRACTION WAS RELIABLE. Union 372 rows over 82 files at 86.3 percent `unstated` (82.4 to 89.4), with only 28 of 82 files stating provenance at all and the top 8 files carrying 26 of 51 explicit rows; injected-finding severity is INDISTINGUISHABLE from base (18.2 percent against 21.6 percent medium-or-worse). Inter-rater agreement on the 18-file overlap after normalising id spellings: verdict 91.7 percent (kappa 0.822), class 91.7 (0.876), PROVENANCE 97.2 (0.897), severity 95.8 (0.892), all four simultaneously 80.6. ALL SIX VERDICT DISAGREEMENTS SIT IN ONE FILE and provenance disagreements number exactly two, so disagreement is CONCENTRATED rather than scattered, which is the good shape. BOTH RATERS INDEPENDENTLY FOUND THE SAME SIX `introduced_by_prior_fix` ROWS. HONEST CAVEAT THE ANALYST RAISED AGAINST ITSELF: extractor A never states its model in its own record, so the cross-model claim rests on this ledger's word rather than on the artifact.

Q6, AND TWO MORE CORRECTIONS, ONE OF THEM TO THE ORCHESTRATOR'S OWN ADJUDICATION. EACH EXTRACTOR HAD EXACTLY ONE FALSE POSITIVE: A's round 202 is false (which the orchestrator caught), and B'S `user-prompts-dir` IS ALSO FALSE AND NOBODY CAUGHT IT, since `user-prompts-dir-triage.md` exists at corpus index 68 inside A's own slice. THE ORCHESTRATOR'S DIAGNOSIS OF THE CAUSE WAS WRONG: it recorded a token-slot drift, but the real cause is a NAMESPACE COLLISION BETWEEN TWO REVIEW LOOPS SHARING ONE STEP SLUG. VERIFIED DIRECTLY: step 93 has FIVE triage files, not the four this ledger claimed, because BOTH `-r3-triage.md` (deleted, and its own header opens "Plan review round 3, triage") AND `-triage-r3.md` (live, work review round 3) exist, while `-triage.md` is ambiguous between the two loops. THE SHIPPED CONVENTION `<step>-<role>-<disambiguator>` HAS NO PHASE SLOT AT ALL, and that, not the round-token position, is the defect. THE TRUE SHORTFALL IS ABOUT 38 ROUNDS (32 to 49), roughly 35 PERCENT of finding-bearing rounds, against the 13 pre-registered here; its largest cause is multi-round loops carrying ONE triage file for SEVERAL finding-bearing rounds, which fits NEITHER pre-registered cause.

THE `valid_findings` DEFECT IS CONFIRMED THREE WAYS AND IS WORSE THAN REPORTED. Six rounds are recorded `clean` while carrying `valid_findings > 0`, proving residuals ARE counted there, and both extractors independently found the same two overlap files where they are NOT, which is cross-model replication of the inconsistency; the rate is 8 of 51 (15.7 percent, 8.1 to 28.3). THE ANALYST FOUND A FURTHER DEFECT NEITHER EXTRACTOR DID: 22 of 147 rounds have `sum(reviewers[].valid_findings) != valid_findings`, and THREE OF THOSE ARE HIGHER, which deduplication cannot explain. CONSEQUENCE, STATED PLAINLY BECAUSE IT INVALIDATES FIGURES THIS LEDGER PUBLISHED: every COUNT-SUMMING result is unreliable, INCLUDING the 419 total and BOTH versions of the severity mix (the 22-versus-25 and the 23.0-versus-24.0 corrections alike), while every OUTCOME-BASED result stands, since `outcome` is recorded independently of the counts. So the 54.1 percent and 14.3 percent rates survive and the "extra rounds are not producing junk" conclusion now rests on the Q1 severity inventory rather than on the mix. ONE CAVEAT THE ANALYST RAISED AGAINST ITS OWN Q1: round index 146 is one of the 22 enforced rounds and is recorded `clean` while its triager ruled two low findings valid, so 0-of-22 is a bound on WHAT GOT RECORDED and recording is lossy DOWNWARD.

THE INTERPRETIVE EXTRACTION RAN (2026-07-31) AND ITS LOAD-BEARING COLUMN IS 86 PERCENT EMPTY, WHICH IS ITSELF THE ANSWER TO A DIFFERENT QUESTION. Records at `docs/plans/workflow-calibration.explorations/finding-provenance-extract-{a,b}.md` with datasets alongside. THE TWO EXTRACTORS AGREE CLOSELY ON THE NUMBER THAT MATTERS, having never seen each other's work: A extracted 221 rows with `unstated` 191 (86.4 percent), `pre_existing` 16, `introduced_by_prior_fix` 14; B extracted 225 rows with `unstated` 193 (86 percent), `pre_existing` 18, `introduced_by_prior_fix` 14. BOTH INDEPENDENTLY FOUND THAT 13 OF THE 14 `introduced_by_prior_fix` ROWS ARE ABOUT PROSE rather than code, which is the first corpus-wide support for a pattern this ledger had recorded only as five anecdotes. NEITHER CAN SUPPORT AN INJECTION RATE and both said so unprompted: at 86 percent unstated the denominator is unobserved, and the recorded cases CLUSTER IN THE FEW LOOPS WHOSE TRIAGERS HAPPENED TO WRITE PROVENANCE SECTIONS, so where injection HAPPENED cannot be distinguished from where it was WRITTEN DOWN.

THE MOST VALUABLE FINDING IS A SCHEMA FINDING, AND IT INVALIDATES THE ORCHESTRATOR'S PRE-REGISTERED VOCABULARY A SECOND TIME. Extractor A found that THE MOST COMMON EXPLICIT CAUSATION RULING IN THE CORPUS FITS NEITHER POSITIVE VALUE: fourteen times a triager ruled, in quotable words, that a defect is NEW CONTENT OF THE ARTIFACT UNDER REVIEW, neither pre-existing nor attributable to a prior fix. A recorded all fourteen as `unstated`, correctly, because the pre-registered vocabulary had nowhere to put them. SO THE THREE-VALUE VOCABULARY WOULD HAVE SILENTLY DISCARDED THE CATEGORY TRIAGERS ACTUALLY RECORD MOST, and the forward instrumentation needs FOUR values: `introduced_by_prior_fix`, `pre_existing`, `new_content_of_artifact`, `unstated`. NOTE WHAT THIS DOES TO THE ORIGINAL QUESTION: `new_content_of_artifact` IS the new-content hypothesis, and triagers have been ruling it explicitly all along, so among findings where causation was recorded at all the three substantive categories are roughly comparable in size rather than one dominating. That is suggestive and it is NOT a rate, for the clustering reason above.

A DATA-QUALITY DEFECT IN THE ROUND LOG ITSELF, FOUND BY EXTRACTOR A: `valid_findings` IS NOT CONSISTENT ABOUT WHETHER ACCEPTED RESIDUALS COUNT. A found accepted residuals INCLUDED in the count at some rounds and EXCLUDED at eight others, every one of its eight reconciliation mismatches being a finding ruled valid but resolved without a fix. SO THE FIELD CARRIES TWO DIFFERENT MEANINGS ACROSS THE 204 ROUNDS, which does not affect the outcome-level figures (`clean` versus `new_valid`, and therefore the 54.1 and 14.3 percent rates) but DOES undermine any analysis that sums or compares finding COUNTS across rounds. This is a genuine instrumentation defect and belongs in `workflow-calibration`'s design pass alongside the four-value vocabulary.

THE TWO EXTRACTORS DISAGREE ON THE SHORTFALL, AND THE ORCHESTRATOR ADJUDICATED IT. A reported 10 rounds with no surviving triage file, identified by round index, INCLUDING round 202; B reported 9 tasks with none, NOT including step 93. VERIFIED DIRECTLY: round 202 is a step-93 round and step 93 has ALL FOUR of its triage files committed, so A's list contains at least one FALSE POSITIVE and B is right on this point. THE PLAUSIBLE CAUSE IS THIS PROJECT'S OWN RECORDED FINDINGS-FILE NAMING DRIFT: step 93's four triage files are named `-triage.md`, `-r2-triage.md`, `-triage-r3.md` and `-triage-r4.md`, so the round-2 file puts its round token in a DIFFERENT SLOT from the round-3 and round-4 files, and a matcher expecting one shape misses the other. THIS IS THE FIRST MEASURED DOWNSTREAM COST OF THAT DRIFT, which was previously recorded as harmless because it caused no collisions; it is now known to corrupt a round-to-file join. The shipped convention `<step>-<role>-<disambiguator>` should be enforced, not merely stated.

BOTH EXTRACTORS ALSO INDEPENDENTLY FOUND THE SAME CORPUS DEFECT, which is a good sign about the method: the case-insensitive `triage` filter also matches `triageR`, admitting reviewer-claim files from the `triager-independence` and `triager-on-findings` artifacts. Both kept them in the derivation (so the pre-registered 89 stays reproducible) and both extracted ZERO rows from them, per the rule that this pass extracts rulings and not claims. Neither was told about it.

THE INTERPRETIVE PASS IS PRE-REGISTERED HERE, BEFORE ANY EXTRACTOR LOOKS AT THE CORPUS (2026-07-31), and it is now THE CRITICAL PATH rather than a supplement, for the reason Explorer A established below. TWO HUMAN DECISIONS: (1) run it as a SPLIT CORPUS WITH A SHARED OVERLAP, two extractor explorers on different models each covering about half, plus a shared sample both extract so inter-rater agreement is MEASURED rather than assumed, then a SEPARATE ANALYST that never saw the extraction; (2) the forward-instrumentation gap (record the fix-pass commit range on the round record) is FOLDED INTO `workflow-calibration`'s own design pass rather than made a separate schema change, because that step already owns per-finding identity as its first design question and two separate edits to the metrics schema that W3/W4/W5 read would be worse than one.

THE CORPUS AND ITS SPLIT, DEFINED REPRODUCIBLY SO NEITHER EXTRACTOR DEPENDS ON A FILE THE ORCHESTRATOR HAND-BUILT. All files that have ever existed under `docs/plans/*.reviews/` number 343 (`git log --diff-filter=D --name-only` for the deleted ones, `git ls-files` for the live ones, sorted unique). OF THOSE, 89 ARE TRIAGE FILES AND 254 ARE REVIEWER FILES, AND THE EXTRACTION TAKES THE 89 TRIAGE FILES ONLY. That is not a cost compromise, it is the correctness discipline already recorded above applied literally: PREFER TRIAGER VERDICTS OVER REVIEWER CLAIMS, because the reviewer files contain known errors that triagers caught and reproduced, so extracting what was RULED rather than what was CLAIMED inherits the adjudication instead of the noise. THE SPLIT RULE, deterministic over the sorted triage list: index mod 5 == 0 is the SHARED OVERLAP (18 files, 20.2 percent) extracted by BOTH; of the remainder, even index goes to extractor A and odd to extractor B, giving A 54 files and B 53.

THE EXPECTED SHORTFALL, STATED IN ADVANCE SO IT IS NOT DISCOVERED AS A SURPRISE: there are 89 triage files against 102 `new_valid` rounds (99 changed plus 3 unchanged), so roughly 13 rounds with findings have NO triage file. Two causes are already known and both are historical: the earliest rounds predate the findings-files convention (`Q-14`), and some early rounds collapsed triage into the producer or the orchestrator, which is exactly the defect `Q-63` later closed by requiring a separate triager on any round with at least one finding. THE EXTRACTORS MUST REPORT THE ACTUAL SHORTFALL AND ITS PER-ROUND IDENTITY rather than absorbing it, and the analyst must treat pre-`Q-63` rounds as a distinct stratum, since a round triaged by its own producer is not evidence of the same quality as one triaged independently.

THE MECHANICAL ARCHAEOLOGY RAN AND ITS PRIMARY MEASUREMENT IS UNMAKEABLE (2026-07-31). TWO EXPLORERS ON DIFFERENT MODELS (`claude-opus-5` and `claude-sonnet-5`), working independently in isolated worktrees from the pre-registration at `12d6a01`, BOTH CONCLUDED THE PRE-REGISTERED PRIMARY MEASUREMENT CANNOT BE MADE, for overlapping but distinct reasons. Records at `docs/plans/workflow-calibration.explorations/fix-pass-shape-mechanical-{a,b}.md` with their datasets alongside as TSV. THIS IS A USEFUL RESULT, NOT A FAILED RUN: the reasons are themselves the finding, and three of them are defects in the instrumentation or in the pre-registration rather than in the data.

DEFECT IN THE PRE-REGISTRATION ITSELF, FOUND BY EXPLORER A, AND IT INVALIDATES THE DESIGN RATHER THAN THE DATA. The pre-registered primary measurement was P(next round is `new_valid` | fix-pass shape). `new_valid` IS A POOR PROXY FOR INJECTION, because a round is `new_valid` whenever it finds ANY valid finding, including PRE-EXISTING ones the previous rounds walked past. On the ONE case where both signals exist the two disagree IN SIGN: commit `3e4fb6c`, the ledger's flagship deletion-class pass, was followed by a round recorded `new_valid`, while this ledger's own text records those 2 findings as PRE-EXISTING and explicitly not re-seeds. SO NO SHAPE-VERSUS-OUTCOME TABLE COULD EVER HAVE MEASURED THE INJECTION RATE, at any sample size and with perfect data. THE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE DESIGN: causation must be read from TRIAGER RULINGS, which state it explicitly, and cannot be inferred from round outcomes. The interpretive pass over the 296 recoverable review files is therefore not a supplement to the mechanical pass, it is THE ONLY PATH to the injection rate, and the orchestrator's pre-registration was wrong to frame it as secondary.

"DELETION-ONLY" MUST BE A TOKEN PREDICATE, NOT A LINE PREDICATE, FOUND BY EXPLORER A AND VERIFIED. Under a line-based classifier ZERO of 32 locatable fix passes are deletion-only, and this is not an aggregation artifact (19 of 19 single-commit windows are `mixed` too). THE CAUSE: deleting a clause from a WRAPPED doc comment REFLOWS the surviving text, so a pure deletion still shows added lines. Re-measured on `3e4fb6c`, the diff is MINUS 3 PLUS 2 LINES while the token multiset shows exactly ONE new token (`self-extending.` displacing `self-extending:`), which reproduces this ledger's own hand-count EXACTLY. So this project's own five-for-five deletion claim was always a token-level claim, and any future classifier must measure tokens. NOTE THE IRONY WORTH KEEPING: the no-hard-wrap convention this project adopted for prose is what makes line-level diff classification useless on its own artifacts.

THE COMMIT-WINDOW METHOD IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND HERE, FOUND BY EXPLORER B, AND THE CAUSE IS THIS PROJECT'S OWN ISOLATION DISCIPLINE. 83 of 115 round-transition pairs are CO-RECORDED (rounds K and K+1 blame to the identical commit), so the window is empty by construction. The structural reason is that worktree-isolated implementer branches MERGE IN A LATE UNDIFFERENTIATED BATCH that lands AFTER a loop's round-recording commits rather than between them. EXPLORER A INDEPENDENTLY HIT THE SAME WALL FROM THE OTHER SIDE and recorded the cure: a main-line `git diff` between round-recording commits is WRONG here, because fix passes are authored in isolated worktrees, some rebased, some squashed, one still unmerged, and under main-line ancestry 27 of 38 windows measured EMPTY against a log that said the artifact changed; using AUTHOR DATE over `git log --all --no-merges` recovers the true work order (rebase preserves author date) and brought that disagreement to ZERO. THE STANDING LESSON: THE WORKFLOW'S OWN WRITER-ISOLATION RULE DESTROYS THE TIMELINE ITS CALIBRATION NEEDS, and the fix is forward-looking instrumentation (record the fix-pass commit range on the round record) rather than cleverer archaeology.

WHAT DID SURVIVE, AND WHAT IT DOES AND DOES NOT SUPPORT. The one association Explorer A could measure is SIZE, NOT SHAPE: median `new_tokens` 761.5 before a `new_valid` round (n=14) against 88 before a `clean` one (n=13), Mann-Whitney AUC 0.788, p about 0.011, which does NOT clear Bonferroni at 5 pre-registered tests and which is CONSISTENT WITH BOTH HYPOTHESES, so it does not separate them. Two signals point WEAKLY AWAY from injection and are handed to the interpretive pass to confirm or kill: the `new_valid` rate DOES NOT DECAY with loop length (38/47/43/59/54 percent across 1/2/3/4-5/6+ round artifacts, all 204 rounds, no exclusions), and in the artifact where fix-pass size collapses hardest (`prompt-drift-guard-inc1`, at 575, 6683, 95, 1, 1 new tokens) ROUNDS 4 AND 5 STILL RETURNED `new_valid` after 95-token and 1-token passes. Explorer A's exclusions are 83 of 115 pairs (72.2 percent), all co-recorded, and CHRONOLOGICALLY BIASED LATE (no pair excluded after 2026-07-26), which makes the past-5-rounds-versus-2-rounds question unusable at only 3 of 36 two-round artifacts. Explorer B's exploratory parent-commit heuristic recovered 17 more fix passes, all `mixed` and all followed by `clean`, and B then PROVED that sample selection-biased (it can only recover loop-closing transitions) and declined to report it as a rate. Both explorers refused to manufacture a conclusion, which is the behaviour the brief asked for and the opposite of the failure mode it warned about.

RECONCILIATION, AND A CORRECTION TO A FIGURE THIS ORCHESTRATOR PUBLISHED. Explorer A reconciled EXACTLY, re-deriving every ledger-published figure to the digit: 204 rounds, `sum(valid_findings)` = 419 = `sum(len(severities))` with 0 per-round mismatches, severities 1 critical / 12 high / 85 medium / 321 low, changed 99/183 = 54.1 percent, unchanged 3/21 = 14.3 percent, 79 artifacts at 57 `low_risk` and 22 `risky`. Explorer B FLAGGED ONE AGGREGATE THAT DID NOT RECONCILE rather than explaining it away, reporting 265/154 against the ledger's 250/169 with both summing to 419. THE ORCHESTRATOR RE-DERIVED AND EXPLORER B IS RIGHT ON THE DATA WHILE EXPLORER A IS RIGHT ABOUT THE RULE: the split is DEFINITION-DEPENDENT and the ledger's 250/169 came from attributing each artifact its LAST round's `risk_class` (identical here to any-round-risky), while a PER-ROUND attribution, which is more faithful to how the field is actually recorded, gives 265/154 and a medium-or-worse mix of 23.0 percent `low_risk` against 24.0 percent `risky`. THE SUBSTANTIVE CONCLUSION IS UNCHANGED AND SLIGHTLY STRENGTHENED, since 23.0 against 24.0 is even closer than 22 against 25, so "the extra rounds on risky artifacts are not producing junk" holds under either rule. THE DEFECT WAS PUBLISHING A RULE-DEPENDENT NUMBER WITHOUT NAMING THE RULE, which is the same species as orchestrator defect (4).

A POSITIVE FINDING NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR: THE ANTI-DRIFT RULE HAS NEVER BEEN VIOLATED. Exactly TWO artifacts carry more than one `risk_class` across their rounds, `structured-skeleton` and `task-entry-regrounding`, and BOTH ARE UPGRADES from `low_risk` to `risky`, never downgrades. `task-entry-regrounding` is the clearest: it reached streak 1 as `low_risk`, which would have CONVERGED it, was upgraded to `risky` instead, and then earned streak 2. The rule that risk class is fixed at loop-open exists to stop an orchestrator DOWNGRADING to converge, and across 79 artifacts and 204 rounds that has not happened once, while the conservative direction has been taken twice. This is the first quantitative check of that guard on real data and it passes.

`Q-55` IS DECIDED AND FOLDED BUT THE FOLD IS UNMERGED AND UNREVIEWED, AND THREE FURTHER HUMAN DECISIONS LANDED ON TOP OF IT (2026-07-31). READ THIS BEFORE TOUCHING THE `Q-55` WORK.

STATE: the planner's fold is committed on branch `plan/q55-enforcement` at `b3bc8e6` in worktree `.claude/worktrees/plan-q55`, NOT MERGED and NOT REVIEWED, carrying a new `[[step]]` `workflow-enforcement-tier` at order 94 with `[step.provenance]` on `Q-55`, its sidecar, `Q-55` flipped to `decided` with `folded_into`, and all three validators green there (93 steps, 5 open questions). MAIN carries only the two `type:"decision"` receipts (records 234 and 235, `q_id` `Q-55` and `Q-55-scope`). THE FOLD IS NOW KNOWN TO BE INCOMPLETE, so do NOT review or merge it as it stands; it needs a SECOND PLANNER PASS first, per the decisions below.

DECISION 1 (human, 2026-07-31): THE METRICS-PATH MECHANISM IS NOT THE IMPLEMENTER'S TO CHOOSE. The planner had left it open with four candidates ((a) anchor the default to the plan source's project root, (b) detect and refuse a cross-root pairing, (c) require an explicit `--metrics`, (d) let `[meta]` declare the plan's own log path), citing the `checks-runner-worktree-name-collision` precedent where an implementer chose between designs and measured the choice. THE HUMAN REJECTED THAT ROUTING and asked how the decision should be made. THE ANSWER, and it is the project's own rule rather than a fresh invention: this has the `Q-69` SIGNATURE of options authored BEFORE THE DESIGN SPACE WAS MAPPED (four candidates, two with unresolved sub-questions, one that may belong to a different step), and `pack/AGENTS.md:45` reserves exactly that case for an `exploring` item plus EXPLORERS. `Q-69` is the recorded cost of doing otherwise: two consecutive rounds found valid defects in its option set's PREMISES rather than its prose, each invisible to the round before, and step 91 built on those options had to be DELETED. SO A DESIGN PASS IS OWED, and the human chose the UPGRADED form: the explorers must BUILD their candidate in a scratch copy and MEASURE it rather than writing prose for it, on this task's five-for-five evidence that a prescription is unsound until someone builds it and that three bad ones were caught only by triagers who did. NOT YET STARTED. Records should go under `docs/plans/workflow-enforcement-tier.explorations/` (directory not yet created; the orchestrator creates it when explorers run, same shape as `Q-52` and `Q-68`).

DECISION 2 (human, 2026-07-31): BOTH SCOPE ADDITIONS THE PLANNER DECLINED TO WIDEN INTO ARE IN. (i) THE SE-3 DOCUMENTATION HALF: `Q-55`'s own text says the two-tier split is undocumented in the scaffolded `AGENTS.md`, so a user reading the role prompt expects guarantees they do not get; including it means the step also edits PACK content and regenerates deployed files, which brings the drift guard into play. (ii) THE SIBLING COMMANDS: `status` (`src/main.rs:455-457`), `next` (`:479-481`) and `default_ledger_path` (`:1133-1138`) share the IDENTICAL cwd-relative default; the planner judged them separable because a wrong path there yields an empty projection rather than a false assertion, but it is the same bug and the human chose to fix it once rather than three times. CONSEQUENCE: the step grows from TWO increments to roughly FOUR and the second planner pass must re-derive the increment set and re-justify each risk class, NOT merely append.

THE PLANNER'S OWN FINDINGS, WHICH SHARPEN DEFECT B AND MUST SURVIVE INTO THE NEXT PASS. It turned the "empty green" into a MEASURED FALSE PASS rather than an absence: give the scratch plan a step slug that exists in THIS repo with round records (it used `triager-runs-only-on-findings`) and mark it `complete`, run from the agent-scaffold root, and the tool prints `workflow invariants hold` at exit 0, so AGENT-SCAFFOLD'S OWN ROUNDS SATISFIED A FOREIGN PROJECT'S CONVERGENCE CLAIM. Control: the same plan with an EMPTY `docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl` inside the fixture FAILS with the W3 message at exit 1, which also establishes that an empty-but-present log is NOT a false green and that the decided cut at "missing file" is the right one. Cause: `w3_problems` joins rounds to steps BY SLUG ALONE (`src/workflow.rs:448-449`) with NO PROJECT IDENTITY anywhere in the record. IT ALSO CORRECTED THE ORCHESTRATOR'S REPRODUCTION: both skip notes go to STDERR, not stdout (`eprintln!` at `src/main.rs:845` and `:1001-1003`), which STRENGTHENS the case since a CI gate reads the exit status. AND IT FOUND THE IN-TREE PRECEDENT FOR DEFECT A'S REMEDY: `src/main.rs:995-998` already hard-errors the sibling arm with the reason "skipping would green-pass while checking nothing", pinned by `tests/validate_workflow_toml_source_needs_no_plan.rs:89-132` whose comment at `:96-98` explicitly records the gap this step closes.

A PLANNER DEVIATION THE ORCHESTRATOR ACCEPTS AND THE NEXT PASS SHOULD FOLLOW: it did NOT write into `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.questions/Q-55.md`, because ALL 69 question sidecars in this project are 0 BYTES, question prose lives in the TOML `ask` field, and every decided question (`Q-52`, `Q-57`, `Q-59`, `Q-60`) records its decision paragraphs there; writing a body would have created a brand-new `## Question Details` section containing only `Q-55` (`question_details_section`, `src/plan/render.rs:564-575`). The decision, both rejected alternatives, the "silently passes" correction and defect B all went into `Q-55`'s `ask` instead. IF THE SIDECAR CONVENTION IS EVER CHANGED, change it deliberately and for all 69, not incidentally for one.

`Q-55` REPRODUCED BY ACTUALLY RUNNING IT (2026-07-31), AND THERE ARE TWO DEFECTS, NOT ONE. The second is NOT in `Q-55`'s text, is worse than the one that is, and was found only because the orchestrator scaffolded a throwaway non-instrumented project and ran the tool against it instead of quoting the audit. METHOD, reproducible: `scaffold --output-dir <scratch> --write --force --principles default` with NO `--instrument`, which drops 30 files and NO `docs/metrics/` directory, then `validate --source <scratch>/docs/plans/TEMPLATE.plan.toml --workflow` run two ways.

DEFECT 1, `Q-55` AS DESCRIBED, CONFIRMED BUT ITS WORDING NEEDS CORRECTING. Run from INSIDE the scratch project, the tool prints `no metrics log at docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl; nothing to validate` and `--workflow has a plan source but the metrics log is missing; skipping the workflow check`, then EXITS 0. So `Q-55`'s "silently passes" is an OVERSTATEMENT, the skip IS announced on stdout; the operative defect is the EXIT CODE, because a CI gate on `validate --workflow` goes green for a project with zero machine enforcement of any workflow invariant. The false green is real, the silence is not.

DEFECT 2, NOT IN `Q-55`, AND IT IS CROSS-PROJECT CONTAMINATION RATHER THAN A MISSING CHECK. The metrics-log path is resolved RELATIVE TO THE CURRENT WORKING DIRECTORY, not relative to the plan source. Run from inside agent-scaffold against the scratch project's plan, the tool reported `docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl: 233 records, valid` and then `<scratch>/docs/plans/TEMPLATE.plan.toml vs docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl: workflow invariants hold`, EXIT 0. Those 233 records are AGENT-SCAFFOLD'S OWN LOG, joined against a foreign 1-step plan, and the join was declared to hold. SO THE GREEN IS NOT MERELY EMPTY, IT IS AFFIRMATIVELY WRONG: it asserts an invariant over a pairing of two unrelated projects. This is a straight bug independent of whichever tier policy is chosen, and it should be fixed regardless.

STEP 93 IS COMPLETE AND MERGED (2026-07-31), AND THE CALIBRATION'S PREDICTION HELD EXACTLY. THE VERIFICATION ROUND (record 233) WAS CLEAN, ZERO FINDINGS FROM BOTH LENSES on two different models, so no triage step was owed. Implementer commit `596548b`, 19 insertions and 9 deletions in `src/checks.rs`, ZERO PRODUCTION LINES CHANGED (`claim_dir` itself byte-identical); ff-merged to main; post-merge 386 tests pass across seven binaries with clippy silent, verified by the orchestrator on the main tree. Step status flipped to `complete`, `[[step.increment]]` `checks-runner-worktree-name-collision-inc1` declared at `risk_class` `risky`, and the convergence shortfall waived as `[[step.waiver]]` id `checks-runner-worktree-name-collision-w1`, `unit` `increment`, `reason` `accepted-at-escalation`, `evidence_tier` `record-backed`, evidence joining to escalation record 232. The plan now reads 63 complete and 21 waivers, 6 of them `accepted-at-escalation`.

THE PREDICTION AND THE OUTCOME, WORTH RECORDING TOGETHER BECAUSE THIS IS THE FIRST TIME THIS PROJECT MADE A CALIBRATED FORECAST ABOUT ITS OWN LOOP AND THEN CHECKED IT. The analyst said one verification round would suffice at the severity level that matters (0 of 22 enforced second rounds ever found medium-or-worse, upper bound 14.9 percent), and raised its confidence from MEDIUM to HIGH conditional on the fix set being cut to the two pure deletions, on the ground that 20 of 22 recorded injections corpus-wide are prose. The set was cut, the round ran, and it came back clean from both lenses. ONE CONFIRMATION IS NOT A VALIDATION and the recorded thresholds still stand (20 more zero-event enforced rounds would justify lowering the `risky` bar to 1; any single medium-or-worse settles it at 2 permanently), but the deletion-preference rule now has a prospective success alongside its five retrospective ones.

BOTH VERIFICATION LENSES WENT BEYOND THEIR BRIEFS IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION. The region lens ran a mutation NOBODY ASKED FOR (`Err(_) => Err(AlreadyExists)`) to establish that the new `assert_ne!` is LOAD-BEARING rather than decorative, and a fourth under `--no-fail-fast` proving the `372` counts are a fail-fast artefact rather than a coverage loss. It also corroborated the fix arithmetically: the authorised three-fix set measured 23/12, the dropped `RG4` fix measured 4/3, and 23-4=19 with 12-3=9 matches the landed diff exactly, which is an independent check that the commit is fixes 1 and 2 AND NOTHING ELSE. It proved "zero words authored" MECHANICALLY with `git diff --word-diff=plain`, the complete insertion set at both prose sites being one word plus four comment markers. The re-seed lens independently confirmed the `#[test]` count is 33 at BOTH revisions, proving no new test function was added, and ran a probe establishing the real error kind is `NotADirectory` so the new assertion is not a vacuous comparison.

THE NEAR-MISS THE REGION LENS CORRECTLY DECLINED TO RAISE, recorded because the judgement is the interesting part. `NF1`: `src/checks.rs:445-447` says "both outcomes are pinned directly by a test" and the test header says "Both outcomes matter", while the fix's own comment fifteen lines below says "The THIRD outcome this documents". The lens ruled this an INCOMPLETE ENUMERATION rather than a false statement, since in both sentences the antecedent of "both" is the won/lost exclusivity pair and both of those genuinely are pinned, and it distinguished the case explicitly from its predecessor lens's two misses, which were a flatly false clause and an unpinned arm. It marked its own suggested remedy UNMEASURED and said why a green would prove nothing. THAT IS THE DISCIPLINE WORKING: the lens that was told a predecessor's zero had been wrong did not overcorrect into manufacturing a finding.

ORCHESTRATOR CONDUCT NOTE, THE SAME GAP HIT A SECOND TIME. Authoring the `[[step.waiver]]` directly on main is STILL not among the four direct-on-main edits the generated `ISOLATION_POLICY_FRAGMENT` enumerates, and this is the second step to need it. The orchestrator proceeded on the ground that escalation record 232 records the human explicitly authorising "merge with a waiver", so writing the block EXECUTES A DECIDED INSTRUCTION rather than authoring new content; that reading is defensible but it is a reading, and the list should be fixed rather than re-interpreted each time. ADDED TO THE PLANNER WORK OWED: update the closed list to cover waiver authoring, or state explicitly that it routes elsewhere. Note also that the first attempt FAILED VALIDATION for a missing `id` field, caught before commit because the validators run first, which is the discipline working as intended.

STEP 93'S ESCALATION IS DECIDED AND THE HOLD IS LIFTED (human, 2026-07-31, escalation record 232, `human_decision` `decision`). THIS SUPERSEDES THE HOLD PARAGRAPH BELOW. THE DECISION: apply ONLY THE TWO PURE DELETIONS and ACCEPT `RG4`'s narrowing clause AS A RESIDUAL, then ONE VERIFICATION ROUND, then merge with a waiver. WHICH BRANCH OF THE COUNTER RULE APPLIES, stated explicitly because the two branches give different bars and step 92's record shows the trap: this is the "send it back for a specific fix that closes this loop" branch, so the counters RETIRE rather than reset. The RESET branch would be strictly worse here, as the analyst independently confirmed: resetting plus one authorised round ends at streak 1 of 2 and converges NOTHING.

WHY THE FIX SET WAS REDUCED, AND IT IS AN EVIDENCE DECISION RATHER THAN A SHORTCUT. The analyst stated a conditional against its own primary recommendation: at the full three-fix set its confidence in one verification round is MEDIUM, but "if step 93's fix set were cut to the two pure deletions only, I would raise that to HIGH". The ground is that `RG4`'s narrowing clause is THE FIX SET'S ONLY AUTHORED PROSE, and 20 of the 22 recorded injections corpus-wide are `class = prose` (90.9 percent, 72.2 to 97.5). `RG4` IS THEREFORE AN ACCEPTED RESIDUAL, DO NOT RE-RAISE IT: it is a `low` over-claim in a comment stating that widening the prune by prefix would close a window it cannot reach for an orphan recorded under a different `TMPDIR`, the sentence it sits in uniquely carries the Principle 18 least-authority reason the module declines to widen the prune, and the routed-out temp-dir step inherits that sentence anyway. `AD1b`, `X1`, `X5`, `X6`, `X7` and `RG3` remain accepted residuals from earlier rounds on the same terms.

THE VERIFICATION ROUND'S STANDING, so nobody mistakes it for a fresh loop: it is a CHECK ON THAT SPECIFIC REDUCED FIX, asking only whether the two deletions landed as authorised and whether anything was re-seeded, NOT the first round of a new convergence streak. A clean result MERGES. The convergence shortfall is then waived exactly as step 92's was, `unit` `increment`, `reason` `accepted-at-escalation`, `evidence_tier` `record-backed`, `evidence` joining to escalation record 232.

FORWARD INSTRUMENTATION IS SPLIT, CHEAP-DETERMINISTIC NOW AND DESIGN-REQUIRING LATER (human, 2026-07-31). SCHEDULED NOW as mechanical wins that need no design pass and that catch defects already PROVEN to exist: (a) split `valid_findings` from a new `fix_required_findings`, since the field demonstrably carries two meanings at a 15.7 percent inconsistency rate; (b) add `len(severities) == valid_findings` and `sum(reviewers[].valid_findings) >= valid_findings` as DETERMINISTIC validator checks, which would have caught all 22 of the per-reviewer mismatches including the 3 that are inexplicably higher; (c) ENFORCE A PHASE SLOT in findings-file names, since the shipped `<step>-<role>-<disambiguator>` convention has none and two review loops on one step slug have already collided in a way that corrupted a round-to-file join. DEFERRED TO A PLANNER AS ONE STEP, because each needs design: the FOUR-VALUE provenance vocabulary with a scope qualifier and made REQUIRED rather than left a triager habit; recording `fix_pass_commits` and `fix_pass_branch` on the round record, since writer isolation destroys the timeline archaeology needs and left 72 percent of pairs unresolvable; measuring fix shape by TOKEN not line and at per-fix not per-pass granularity; the RANDOMISED post-convergence probe experiment with a pre-registered stopping rule; a post-cap probe on the next three cap-reached risky loops (3 agent runs, settles the cap question); extending `human_decision` to distinguish resume from waiver from send-back, plus the dismissal-recheck record of which ZERO exist; and either stopping the deletion of review files or requiring a machine-readable round-key header before deletion.

WHAT WOULD CHANGE THE RECOMMENDED CONSTANTS, recorded so a future session does not re-litigate from scratch: 20 more zero-event enforced rounds (0 of 42, upper bound 8.4 percent) would justify LOWERING the `risky` bar to 1; ANY SINGLE medium-or-worse finding in an enforced second round settles it at 2 PERMANENTLY; 35 zero-event probes puts the bound under 10 percent and 73 puts it under 5; and three converging post-cap probes out of five would justify raising the cap to 6.

STEP 93 IS HELD, AND `workflow-calibration` IS PULLED FORWARD AHEAD OF IT (HUMAN DECISIONS, 2026-07-31). THIS PARAGRAPH SUPERSEDES THE ESCALATION PARAGRAPH IMMEDIATELY BELOW IT, WHICH REMAINS ACCURATE ABOUT THE ARITHMETIC AND THE AUTHORISED FIX SET BUT IS NO LONGER THE NEXT ACTION. THE IMMEDIATE NEXT ACTION IS `workflow-calibration`'s DESIGN PASS, INCLUDING A GIT-HISTORY ARCHAEOLOGY. Step 93's escalation is NOT abandoned and NOT decided; it is DEFERRED PENDING THE CALIBRATION, with the branch `impl/checks-collision` held ENTIRELY UNTOUCHED at its fully reviewed state (no fix pass, no further rounds), so nothing decays and no agent runs are spent against a bar that is about to be measured. The step's three authorised fixes stay exactly as the round-4 triager built and measured them.

WHY THE HOLD IS NOT A STALL, and what would end it. Step 93's escalation reduces to ONE answerable question: IS A SINGLE VERIFICATION ROUND ENOUGH AFTER A DELETION-CLASS FIX PASS. This task has an unbroken FIVE-FOR-FIVE record that deletion-only fix passes re-seed nothing while prose-authoring passes manufacture the next round's finding, but five anecdotes is not a rate. Across the recoverable corpus it becomes one, and censoring bites far less here than on the general question because step 93's round WILL run, so the quantity in dispute is observable rather than counterfactual. If deletion-class passes show a near-zero injection rate corpus-wide, step 93's fix set (TWO OF THREE PURE DELETIONS) justifies one verification round on evidence rather than by concession; if they inject at a real rate, the `risky` bar is doing genuine work and step 93 gets its two rounds. Either way the escalation stops being a judgement call.

THE MEASUREMENTS THAT DROVE THIS, computed at the escalation from the 204 round records and reproducible from `docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl`. BY RISK CLASS: `low_risk` 57 artifacts, bar 1 clean, MEDIAN 2 rounds, mean excess 0.98, max 5; `risky` 22 artifacts, bar 2 clean, MEDIAN 4 rounds, mean excess 2.14, max 9, with FOUR OF 22 (18 percent) running past the cap of 5. SEVERITY MIX IS NEARLY IDENTICAL ACROSS CLASSES, `low_risk` 55 of 250 findings medium-or-worse (22 percent) against `risky` 43 of 169 (25 percent), so the extra rounds on risky artifacts are NOT producing junk and the "the loop nitpicks risky artifacts" reading is UNSUPPORTED. THE TWO RATES THAT MATTER: on an UNCHANGED artifact a re-review still finds something 3 of 21 times (14.3 percent, 95 percent CI 5.0 to 34.6), while on a CHANGED artifact it is 99 of 183 (54.1 percent, CI 46.9 to 61.2). So one clean round on an unchanged artifact is right about 86 percent of the time and two consecutive gets to about 98 percent IF THE ROUNDS ARE INDEPENDENT, which is the assumption most at risk. CONCLUSION THE DATA SUPPORTS: the 2-consecutive-clean bar is EXPENSIVE BY CONSTRUCTION, which is arithmetic and not miscalibration, and the CAP OF 5 IS INCONSISTENT WITH IT because it sits below where the distribution still has real mass.

THE QUESTION THE EXISTING DATA CANNOT ANSWER, WHICH IS WHY THE ARCHAEOLOGY IS OWED. That 54.1 percent has TWO INCOMPATIBLE EXPLANATIONS the log cannot separate: fix passes INJECT new defects, or changed content is GENUINELY NEW and unreviewed. THE REMEDY IS OPPOSITE IN EACH CASE, since under injection raising the cap makes things WORSE (more rounds, more fixes, more injections) and the fix is to constrain fix passes, while under new-content constraining fixes does nothing and the cap should rise. The round record has NO per-finding identity, NO causation link, NO finding class (prose versus code versus test) and NO record of fix-pass shape; the ledger template states "no cross-round finding identity" as a DELIBERATE design decision. The evidence exists only as prose in reviewer and triage files that are committed then DELETED at step close: 296 such files are recoverable from git history (`git log --diff-filter=D --name-only -- 'docs/plans/*.reviews/*'`), plus the 5 currently live, which is close to every review this project has ever run.

THE ARCHAEOLOGY AS DECIDED (human, 2026-07-31), and the ROLE QUESTION IT LEAVES OPEN. Run it as EXPLORERS with an analysis-specific discipline in the brief, writing under `docs/plans/workflow-calibration.explorations/`, rather than defining a new analyst role in the pack first; the reasoning is that every other role in this pack was adopted after the need RECURRED, not on first sight (Minimal by default), and this run is how we find out whether the discipline is genuinely different. THE HUMAN RAISED A REAL DISTINCTION WORTH KEEPING ON THE RECORD: an analyst's failure mode is SILENT MISCOUNTING, not bad judgement, so it is verified by re-derivation and reconciliation rather than by adversarial re-reading, and this project has already been burned by that failure mode THREE TIMES (the orchestrator's false "first waiver ever" from a grep that matched nothing, a word count recorded as 45-to-43 that was 46-to-42 and propagated through three agents, and a reviewer's 100/100 measurement that re-measured at 10/100). IF THE DISCIPLINE PROVES SUBSTANTIALLY DIFFERENT FROM AN EXPLORER'S, THAT IS THE EVIDENCE FOR ADDING THE ROLE, and it should be raised then rather than assumed now.

WHAT THE ARCHAEOLOGY MUST COLLECT, PER FINDING RATHER THAN PER ROUND, which is the whole point: round identity (step, increment, round number, commit range); finding id, severity AS RAISED, severity AFTER TRIAGE, and verdict (valid, accept-residual, dismissed); CLASS (prose, code, test, config); PROVENANCE, meaning whether the finding was introduced by a prior fix pass in the same loop and if so which round's; FIX-PASS SHAPE for the pass preceding the round (deletion-only versus authored, lines added versus deleted); reviewer attribution (model, lens); and whether the finding ESCAPED and was re-raised in a later round.

HOW ITS CORRECTNESS IS ENFORCED, strongest measure first. THE JSONL IS ALREADY AN ORACLE: every round record independently carries `valid_findings` and `severities` written at the time, so any per-finding extraction MUST RECONCILE TO THOSE NUMBERS EXACTLY across all 204 rounds, and an extraction that does not sum correctly is provably wrong before anyone interprets it. On top of that: TWO INDEPENDENT EXTRACTORS over the same corpus with disagreements treated as signal (the reviewer-diversity principle applied to data); STRUCTURED OUTPUT as TSV or JSON so the analysis is re-runnable rather than asserted (Reproducible, Structured data first); A SEPARATE ANALYST THAT NEVER SAW THE EXTRACTION, so extraction bias cannot leak into interpretation; random spot-checks re-derived from source; and PRE-REGISTERED QUESTIONS decided before looking, because n is small and the temptation to fish is real. ONE DESIGN POINT THAT MATTERS: PREFER TRIAGER VERDICTS OVER REVIEWER CLAIMS, because the findings files contain known errors that triagers caught, so extracting what was RULED rather than what was CLAIMED inherits the adjudication instead of the noise.

THE LIMIT TO HOLD THE ARCHAEOLOGY TO, stated in advance so no one over-reads its output: IT CANNOT FIX CENSORING. It records what rounds that RAN found, never what an unrun round would have found, so it can measure the fix-injection rate and the finding-class mix (both currently unknown) but CANNOT on its own establish the value of the second clean round beyond the 3-of-21 above. That needs a FORWARD EXPERIMENT, occasionally running one extra round past convergence and recording what it finds, which should be built into the calibration step rather than discovered later. The step's own sidecar already frames the clean-round constant as a CENSORING problem needing a sequential or survival framing rather than agreement statistics, and already names per-finding identity as "the enabling schema change" and "the first design question"; what it does NOT contain is the FIX-INJECTION HYPOTHESIS, which is this task's own discovery and must be added to its brief by the planner.

MUTATION-CADENCE GAP, RAISED BY THE HUMAN (2026-07-30) AND DECIDED "RECORD IT, DO NOT SCHEDULE IT". The human asked how this task's mutations are generated, whether a tool such as `cargo-mutants` should be used, and whether new tests should be mutation-tested as a matter of course. THE FACTS, all verified rather than recalled: every mutation in this task is HAND-PICKED and hand-reverted, and `cargo-mutants` appears in neither `flake.nix` nor `.agents/checks.toml`; the `mutation` step (`Q-31`) is `not-started` at ORDER 51 and hard-depends on `test-driven`, also `not-started`, at order 50; `Kind::Mutation` is already a reserved variant at `src/checks.rs:95` returning `Runnable::Skip("mutation checks run in the later mutation module")` at `:671-672`; and `reviewer-reproducible-evidence` (`Q-66`) is `complete` at ORDER 88. THE GAP: `Q-66` shipped 37 steps AFTER `Q-31` was decided and made a mutation demonstration MANDATORY evidence for any behavioural finding, at EVERY round, while `Q-31`'s decided cadence fires ONCE PER STEP AFTER GREEN CONVERGENCE, diff-scoped to the step's changed CODE. Nobody has revisited `Q-31` in that light, so the review loop now performs BY HAND, EVERY ROUND, what the unbuilt module was designed to do once at the end. The question the planner must register carries two sub-items: (a) the CADENCE, per-round-during-review versus once-after-convergence, and (b) the SCOPE, changed code versus newly added tests, which are not the same set (round 3's Fix A is a new test against an error arm, and the human's framing was about validating new TESTS whereas `Q-31` mutates changed CODE and asks whether tests kill it). CALIBRATION FOR WHOEVER DESIGNS THIS, measured against round 3's own findings: a generic body-nulling tool would very likely have found `AD1a` unaided (`claim_dir -> Ok(true)` is essentially the triager's decisive `M1c`), and would have missed `AD2` (a test ASSERTION satisfiable by incidental digits in the path, not a surviving code mutant), `AD3` (a false comment), and ALL FIFTEEN of step 92's findings, every one of which was prose. So the honest estimate is one of four here and zero of fifteen there: a real but PARTIAL win, and it is the PLANNED implementation rather than an alternative to it, since the `mutation` sidecar already names `cargo-mutants` as an example tool. NOT SCHEDULED: the module stays behind the release gates, and the two rejected options are on the record as (i) adding `cargo-mutants` to the devshell now as an interim reviewer aid, declined because it establishes a habit ahead of the design that governs it, and (ii) pulling the module forward, declined because it is two unbuilt modules ahead of three already-queued release gates (Minimal by default). THE FIDELITY LENS RETURNED "EXACT": only the four briefed passages changed, every added actor clause carries the shipped non-trivial qualifier with NO unqualified instance anywhere in the tree, no fragment content copied or enumerated, Socratic branch 3 byte-unchanged, out-of-scope files untouched, and the guidance copy kept its reopening tail while sharing only the actor clause. `diff pack/prompts/orchestrator.md .agents/prompts/orchestrator.md` empty, `cargo test` green, flake did not fire.

`F1-COLDREAD` (`medium`, VALID, FIX REQUIRED) IS THE ROUND'S REAL FINDING, and it is the one the fidelity lens structurally could not catch, because the change was faithful to a brief that was itself insufficient here. At `pack/prompts/orchestrator.md:31` the pre-existing second-person imperative "record the resolved answer as a durable Open-Questions decision" SURVIVED and the routing clause was appended BESIDE it, so the same sentence COMMANDS an act and ROUTES IT AWAY. Because the Open Questions queue IS `[[question]]` entries in the plan TOML, and because the Socratic path has no create-then-decide stage, recording a decided Socratic answer MINTS a `[[question]]`, which is exactly what `Q-67` reserves for the planner. An orchestrator can read "recording" and "the fold" as separate acts, author the `[[question]]` itself, and route only downstream `[[step]]` work, re-permitting the precise misstep this step exists to prevent.

THE TRIAGER REFUSED THE IMPLEMENTER'S COUNTER-ARGUMENT ON EVIDENCE, and the reasoning is worth keeping because both grounds looked plausible. The implementer had deliberately kept the verb, arguing that deleting it would move EVERY fold off the orchestrator including the trivial ones, and that it was matching the structure of `pack/AGENTS.md:43`. Ground one is a FALSE PREMISE: the scope constraint forbids an UNQUALIFIED "the planner authors that fold", so re-voicing the verb while keeping "non-trivial (authoring a `[[question]]` or a `[[step]]`)" verbatim moves no trivial fold anywhere; the implementer conflated "keep the imperative" with "keep trivial folds with the orchestrator", which are two separate choices. Ground two is FALSE AT THE DISPUTED POINT: `pack/AGENTS.md:43` uses three ACTOR-LESS verbs ("becomes", "routed", "edited in") while the prompt kept "record" as a SECOND-PERSON IMPERATIVE. The decisive evidence is structural: the Socratic mode at `:43` has NO create-then-decide stage, whereas the exploration mode at `:45` explicitly does ("records the question ... with status `exploring` ... only then does it move the item to `open`"); and `src/plan/source.rs:314-317` requires `folded_into` when a question is `decided`, so a Socratic recording mints a `[[question]]` AND must name a `[[step]]`. The step's own brief had also forbidden this outcome in advance at its lines 8 and 20. EVERY CITATION IN BOTH FINDINGS FILES REPRODUCED, with no misnumbering for the first time in several rounds.

`F1-FIDELITY` (`low`, VALID BUT ACCEPT RESIDUAL, do not re-raise): the brief at `decision-folder-currency.md:38` claims `.agents/prompts/orchestrator.md` has NO whole-file drift guard and `:30` calls the hand `diff` "the one part of the currency work no test enforces". BOTH ARE NOW STALE, because step 92 landed FIRST and `src/agents_md_drift.rs:415` guards that path via `PROMPT_DEST_PREFIX`. Accepted because the staleness OVER-warns rather than under-warns, is not this change's doing, and the sidecar goes historical when the step closes. NOTE THIS VINDICATES THE HUMAN'S BUILD-ORDER DECISION: step 92 was sequenced before step 90 precisely so this regeneration would be ENFORCED rather than trusted, and it now is.

THE AUTHORISED FIX, single-site and verified by the triager (`grep -rn "record the resolved answer"` finds it in exactly ONE authored source plus its one generated copy; every other hit is a document quoting it). Re-voice branch 2 ONLY, 46 words to 42 (the triager, the implementer and this ledger all first recorded "45 to 43", a hand estimate that the round-2 verification lens caught and two independent recounts confirmed wrong in both directions), a re-voicing rather than new prose: "for a question whose options are already clear, emit the block, and the resolved answer becomes a durable Open-Questions decision whose non-trivial fold (authoring a `[[question]]` or a `[[step]]`) you route to the planner to author rather than editing the plan yourself;". TWO TRAPS THE TRIAGER FLAGGED: (a) NO SEMICOLON inside branch 2, because the three branches are themselves semicolon-delimited and the reviewer's own suggested wording introduced one, so that wording must NOT be taken literally; (b) branch 3 stays BYTE-UNTOUCHED. Regenerate with the render half only; `pack/AGENTS.md` is untouched so the two guidance copies should come back unchanged, and the `diff` must stay empty.

STEP 93 WORK-REVIEW ROUND 4 (2026-07-31, record 231, outcome `new_valid`, 5 raw findings deduplicating to 4, NOTHING dismissed, severities 1 `medium` + 3 `low`, no high or critical so NO backstop; streak STILL 0 of the 2 its `risky` class needs). Implementer commit `3f49012`, `src/checks.rs` only, +31/-4, ZERO PRODUCTION LINES CHANGED, exactly the triager's round-3 measurement; 386 tests pass, clippy 0/0, reconfirmed independently by the orchestrator and by all three agents this round.

THE TWO LENSES CONVERGED ON THE SAME DEFECT FROM DIFFERENT DIRECTIONS AND DIFFERENT MODELS, which is the strongest signal this task has produced and the clearest vindication of the diversity rule. A `claude-opus-5` REGION SWEEP and a `claude-sonnet-5` MUTATION HUNT independently found `claim_dir`'s OWN error arm at `src/checks.rs:452` to be executed by no test, and the triager confirmed them ONE finding rather than assuming it: same line, byte-identical mutation, same fixture shape, same cause. THE CAUSE IS THE PREVIOUS ROUND'S OWN FIX: round 3's `AD1a` pinned the CALLER's `map_err` through the injected `claim` seam, and the seam BY CONSTRUCTION never calls the real `claim_dir`, so the coverage reads complete one level up while the arm one level down stays bare. The triager's own grep found `claim_dir` has exactly TWO test-side call sites, both inside `a_directory_claim_is_exclusive`, driving `Ok(true)` and `Ok(false)`, with NOTHING driving `Err`. Both mutations survive at 386 green and clippy 0/0, and a binary A/B against a `chmod 555` TMPDIR shows the committed build reporting `Permission denied (os error 13)` while the mutant reports `could not reserve a unique runner worktree directory after 16 attempts`, the exact misdiagnosis round 3 believed it had closed.

ROUND 4 VERDICTS. `RG1`/`MU1` (`medium`, VALID, fix required), deduplicated as above. `RG2` (`low`, VALID, fix required, DELETION, SITE LIST EXTENDED BY THE TRIAGER): the seam's doc sentence claims "Every real claim in this repository WINS", falsified deterministically by instrumenting the lost-claim arm, exactly 1 real lost claim per whole-suite run over 5/5 runs at `a_directory_claim_is_exclusive:1705`. THE TRIAGER FOUND THE IDENTICAL FALSE CLAIM A SECOND TIME at `src/checks.rs:1712-1713`, seven lines below a comment asserting the opposite, and noted that fixing only the cited site WOULD HAVE RE-SEEDED ROUND 5. `RG3` (`low`, VALID BUT ACCEPT RESIDUAL): the guard-before-add ordering is unpinned; the region reviewer built a 78-line integration test and argued to accept on COST, and the triager REFUTED that ground (the cheapest form is 40 lines folded into the existing `tests/checks_missing_tmpdir.rs` with no new binary, cheaper than the already-declined `AD1b`) then accepted anyway on VALUE, which is the honest version of the same verdict. `RG4` (`low`, VALID, fix required): Invariant B over-claims that widening the prune by prefix would close the window, which cannot reach an orphan recorded under a DIFFERENT `TMPDIR`.

`RG2` ALSO CAUGHT A TRIAGER ERROR IN THE PREVIOUS ROUND, and it is the non-zero twin of a lesson this project already recorded against the orchestrator. Round 3's triage certified the sentence TRUE at `triage-r3.md:297` using `grep -n "reserve_runner_worktree"`, a pattern that CANNOT MATCH the direct `claim_dir` call that falsifies it, and whose own output contains the concurrent site `:1826` contradicting the sentence's "sequentially". `git log -S` establishes the falsifying test landed at `339d26a` in round 1 while the sentence was authored at `14692f3` in round 2, SO THE SENTENCE WAS FALSE THE DAY IT WAS WRITTEN. The recorded orchestrator lesson was "a grep returning zero is evidence about the PATTERN as much as about the repo"; this is its non-zero twin, A GREP RETURNING HITS PROVES ONLY THAT THE PATTERN MATCHED, NOT THAT THE POPULATION IT WAS MEANT TO STAND FOR WAS COVERED.

THE MINIMAL FIX SET, all built, measured and reverted by the triager: `23 insertions, 12 deletions` in `src/checks.rs`, ZERO production lines, NO new test binary, green at 386 with clippy 0/0 and RED under both `:452` mutations. TWO OF THE THREE ARE PURE DELETIONS. (1) `RG1`/`MU1`: the REGION reviewer's form ADOPTED OVER the mutation reviewer's on measured grounds, 13 lines folded into the existing `a_directory_claim_is_exclusive` against 22 (advertised as "about 18"), no new fixture-name class, and it completes the contract of a test whose own comment already says "Both outcomes matter"; the rejected form adds a 19th fixture-name class that LEAKS on every RED run, measured at 4 leftovers from 4 RED runs. (2) `RG2`: pure deletion at BOTH sites, zero words authored; the triager DECLINED the region reviewer's replacement wording because it authors a fresh claim naming another test, the class this task has measured re-seeding five times, and the surviving sentence at `:1711` already carries the justification. (3) `RG4`: one narrowing clause, the ONLY authored prose in the set, with pure deletion rejected because that sentence uniquely carries the Principle 18 least-authority reason the module declines to widen the prune, which the routed-out step inherits.

THE CLOCK-RATE NON-FINDING WAS ARBITRATED AND THE REGION REVIEWER'S RESTRAINT WAS RIGHT. It measured 0 of 100000 duplicates at two threads against the step brief's 8.7 percent and explicitly DECLINED to raise it, citing this task's four-for-four prescription record. The triager reproduced BOTH: a tight-loop method gives 6.88/7.43/8.73 percent at two threads and 51.5/55.0/67.1 at sixteen, matching the brief, while a per-round barrier gives 0.00 and 0.8 to 2.1 percent, matching the reviewer. Two different experiments, not a conflict; the brief's figures STAND and no record correction is due. This is the first time on this task a reviewer's self-restraint was tested and upheld rather than a prescription being overturned.

ORCHESTRATOR DEFECT (5), recorded like the other four. THE TWO ROUND-4 REVIEWER WORKTREES WERE CREATED NESTED INSIDE THE IMPLEMENTER'S WORKTREE, at `.claude/worktrees/impl-checks-collision/.claude/worktrees/rev93-r4-{a,b}` instead of at the top level, because a `cd` from an earlier Bash call PERSISTED and the `git worktree add` used a RELATIVE path. This is the same shell-cwd-persistence hazard recorded earlier in this task, recurring in a new place. NO HARM RESULTED and that was verified rather than assumed: both worktrees were genuine isolated checkouts of the correct commit `3f49012` on their own branches, `.claude/` is gitignored so the implementer's `git status` stayed empty throughout, and its tree was confirmed byte-clean at `3f49012` after removal. THE CURE, adopted: ALWAYS pass an ABSOLUTE path to `git worktree add`, never a relative one, since a relative path silently resolves against whatever cwd a previous call left behind.

STEP 93 WORK-REVIEW ROUND 3 (2026-07-30, record 230, outcome `new_valid`, 3 raw findings from one lens becoming 4 after a triager split, NOTHING dismissed, severities 1 `medium` + 3 `low`, no high or critical so NO backstop; streak STILL 0 of the 2 its `risky` class needs).
