THIS IS THE FIRST ROUND IN THIS TASK RUN ON TWO DIFFERENT MODELS, correcting the diversity violation recorded against the orchestrator, and THE SPLIT IS THE HEADLINE: the `claude-sonnet-5` fix-verification lens returned ZERO findings, the `claude-opus-5` adversarial lens returned THREE. The triager ruled the zero CORRECT on the three authorised fixes (it reproduced the decisive disclosed-item mutation itself and did not disturb that ruling) but INCOMPLETE on its own "manufacture nothing new" clause, and diagnosed the shape of the gap precisely: the two misses are one ASSERTION three lines below the one it ruled on, and one CLAUSE of the sentence whose other clause it ruled on, so its sweep was over SENTENCES rather than over the region. That is a lens-scope defect, not a model defect, and it is the first evidence this task has that the earlier all-opus rounds were narrower than they were presented as. DO NOT read this as "sonnet is weaker": the fix-verification lens was pointed at three named fixes and answered that question correctly and in depth, including ruling independently on a disclosed self-referential assertion and getting it right.

ROUND 3 VERDICTS. `AD1` was SPLIT by the triager because the halves differ in consequence and in fix cost by an order of magnitude. `AD1a` (`medium`, VALID, fix required): the reservation's claim ERROR arm is executed by no test. The triager confirmed the reviewer's two mutations (`unwrap_or(false)` and bare `?`, both GREEN at 385) but RE-GROUNDED the severity on a mutation NEITHER LENS TRIED, `claim(&path).unwrap_or(true)`, also GREEN at 385, under which the reservation returns a path whose claim was never established, falsifying `src/checks.rs:483-485` ("that outcome, not an entropy argument, is what makes the returned path exclusively ours"). The reviewer's own stated ground, message quality, would have carried only `low`. This is the same class as round 2's `X2` and is NOT covered by it: `X2` pinned a LOST claim and says nothing about an ERRORED one, and round 2's table recorded this arm as not tried. `AD1b` (`low`, VALID BUT ACCEPT RESIDUAL): the temp-dir creation error arm degrades by one noun while both messages still name the path and the same errno and exit 2; pinning it costs a whole new spawned-binary integration test because `std::env::temp_dir()` has no seam, and injecting the temp dir is the second-testability-parameter move round 2 already declined for the clock. `AD2` (`low`, VALID, fix required, FRAMING CORRECTED): `message.contains(&RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS.to_string())` can be satisfied by digits from the path itself. The reviewer's 100/100 spurious-pass figure DID NOT REPRODUCE; the triager re-measured 10 PASS / 90 FAIL over 100 fresh processes and found the digits arriving from the 19-digit nanos rather than the pid, so the assertion does not pin nothing, it pins at a rate that is a property of the developer's pid counter and clock digits, 0 percent on the reviewer's machine and 87 to 90 percent on the triager's. `AD3` (`low`, VALID, fix required, REMEDY CHANGED TO DELETION): the comment written to fix round 2's `X8b` states in the present tense that nothing exercises the lost-claim verdict, the retry, or the exhaustion error and that each can be deleted with a green suite; all three measured false against the tree the comment sits in.

THE MINIMAL FIX SET, all three built, measured and reverted by the triager: 386 passed, clippy 0/0, TEN consecutive full `cargo test` runs green, +31/-4 in `src/checks.rs`, and ZERO PRODUCTION CODE CHANGED. Fix A (`AD1a`): one 27-line unit test on the EXISTING seam, RED under all three claim-error mutations. Fix B (`AD2`): one line, `contains(&RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS.to_string())` -> `contains(&format!("after {RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS} attempts"))`, which moves the measured kill rate from 10/100 to 100/100 while keeping the symbolic constant so the settled `16 -> 3` behaviour is unchanged. Fix C (`AD3`): DELETE the false clause, net -2 lines, ZERO words authored.

PRESCRIPTION TESTED NOT TRUSTED, FOURTH INSTANCE, and this one cut in three directions rather than the usual one. The `AD1` fix was advertised by its author as two tests, 77 lines, 387 passing; BUILT, it is ONE test, 27 lines, 386 passing, and it kills a mutation the reviewer never had, with the reviewer's second test measured as disproportionate and declined. `AD2`'s proposal was RIGHT IN SUBSTANCE but was unbuilt by its author and its headline measurement did not reproduce. `AD3`'s diagnosis was right and its METHOD was wrong: the reviewer prescribed a past-tense rewrite, which the triager declined explicitly as re-seeding, choosing deletion instead. The running tally is now `FN-2` (step 92), round 1's `T1`, round 2's clock parameter, and all three of round 3's.

STEP 93 WORK-REVIEW ROUND 2 (2026-07-30, record 229, outcome `new_valid`, 8 raw findings surviving as 8 after one merge and one split, NOTHING dismissed, severities 3 `medium` + 5 `low`, no high or critical so NO backstop; streak STILL 0 of the 2 its `risky` class needs). ALL FOUR ROUND-1 FIXES LANDED, each verified by mutation rather than by reading. Neither lens could break the uniqueness property, and neither could make the prune reclaim a LIVE worktree.

THE ROUND'S THROUGH-LINE, MEASURED BY THE TRIAGER AND STRONGER THAN EITHER LENS PUT IT: FOUR OF FIVE LOAD-BEARING PARTS CAN EACH BE DELETED ALONE WITH 382 TESTS GREEN AND CLIPPY SILENT, namely the `create_dir_all(&temp)`, the atomic sequence, the `if claimed` verdict, and the retry loop. The suite goes red only when the whole construction goes. TWO RECORDED EXPLANATIONS ARE THEREFORE FALSE AND ARE CORRECTED HERE: the implementer's "the 16 retries absorb the collisions" and round 1's "the retry path carries the property". Instrumentation shows deepest retry depth 1 and 2020 attempts for 2000 reservations WITH AND WITHOUT the layer-1 mutation, so there are ZERO collisions and ZERO retries; with layer 1 disabled AND a panic planted on the lost-claim branch, `checks::` passes 30/30 three times. The property test's redness is carried by the `mkdir` syscall's CLOCK SPACING, not by either layer, and the margin is thin (1 to 15 duplicates per 2000, red 8 of 8).

A THIRD UNSOUND REVIEWER PRESCRIPTION, CAUGHT THE SAME WAY. The verification lens proposed a clock parameter plus a frozen-clock test and claimed it would also close the retry hole. The triager BUILT IT AND MEASURED: green on committed code, RED under `fetch_add` -> `load`, but GREEN under `RUNNER_RESERVE_ATTEMPTS = 1` and GREEN under `if claimed || true`, so its advertised side-benefit is FALSE, because on correct code a frozen clock never loses a claim. The adversarial lens's injectable-claim prescription does work as described and was adopted instead. THAT IS THREE FOR THREE IN THIS TASK: `FN-2` (step 92), the round-1 `T1` proposal, and now this. THE STANDING LESSON, now beyond doubt: A REVIEWER'S DIAGNOSIS AND ITS PRESCRIPTION CARRY DIFFERENT WEIGHT, and a prescription must be executed and measured before it is trusted.

ROUND 2 VERDICTS. `X2` (`medium`, VALID, = adversarial F2 merged with the retry half of verification F1): the loop's collision handling is executed by NOTHING; `if claimed` -> `if claimed || true` at `:524` leaves the full suite, the new exclusivity test AND the property test all green. This is the unclosed remainder of round 1's `T1`. `X3` (`medium`, VALID): the round-1 `TMPDIR` regression fix is pinned by no test; deleting `:501-506` leaves the suite green while `TMPDIR=<missing>` goes exit 0 -> exit 2. `X4` (`medium`, VALID for its PROSE half only): the prune permanently SKIPS A REGISTERED DEAD ORPHAN when git records a symlink-resolved path outside `std::env::temp_dir()`, and both prune tests FAIL under a symlinked `TMPDIR`; the MECHANISM IS PRE-EXISTING (prune body byte-identical `HEAD~2..HEAD`) but the Invariant B text THIS step newly wrote states the bound as registration only, which is incomplete. `X1` (`low`, ACCEPT RESIDUAL WITH THE REASON CORRECTED): layer 1 is a NAME OPTIMISATION and correctness rides on layer 2, which fix 2 pins end to end; the previously recorded reason was wrong and must not be repeated. `X5`, `X6` (`low`, ACCEPT RESIDUAL), `X7` (`low`, record correction only: the commit message quotes an errno the binary does not produce), `X8` (`low`, VALID, two comments this commit wrote are false).

THE AUTHORISED ROUND-2 FIX SET, all three implemented, measured and reverted by the triager (385 green, clippy silent). (1) `X3`: an integration test, NO production change, about 55 lines, green on HEAD and RED with `:501-506` deleted. (2) `X2` + `X8b`: `reserve_runner_worktree_with(pid, claim: impl Fn(&Path) -> io::Result<bool>)`, about 8 production lines plus two tests, RED under `|| true` and RED under attempts=1, with `fetch_add` -> `load` staying green as the DOCUMENTED residual `X1`. (3) `X4` prose + `X8a`: two clauses, Invariant B only (`:42-51`) plus `:495-496`; do NOT touch `:336-339` or `:874-877`, and do NOT widen the prune.

A NEW ROADMAP STEP IS OWED, and it is PLANNER work not yet done: close `X4`'s CODE half and `X6` together, since `std::env::temp_dir()` is consumed raw at `:494` and `:573`. It must canonicalise the prune's gate and reject a relative temp dir, and it NEEDS ITS OWN RISK CLASS because it changes what the prune deletes. The triager endorsed the reviewer's split of prose-here / code-later, and ruled that the tempting "but the suite lies" counter does not carry, since the environment dependence is pre-existing and orthogonal to this step.

FILESYSTEM HYGIENE, worth recording because it is a real cost of adversarial review: a round-2 reviewer's exhaustion-path mutation left 32272 empty `agent-scaffold-checks-run-*` directories in `/tmp` and its own cleanup was refused by the permission classifier, so the ORCHESTRATOR removed them. The round-2 triager was briefed to point its probes at a scratch `TMPDIR` and reported creating 0 in `/tmp`. FUTURE REVIEWER BRIEFS ON THIS STEP MUST CARRY THAT INSTRUCTION.

STEP 93 WORK-REVIEW ROUND 1 (2026-07-30, record 228, outcome `new_valid`, 12 raw findings deduplicated to 10, 8 VALID and 2 DISMISSED, severities 3 `medium` + 5 `low`; NO high or critical raised or dismissed so NO backstop owed; streak 0 of the 2 its `risky` class needs). Implementer commit `b890c4a`, `src/checks.rs` only, +212/-26. IT CHOSE (a)+(d) AND MEASURED THE CHOICE rather than arguing it: an 8-process probe at the constant-pid fixture template gave (a)-alone duplicates in 8 of 8 runs and (a)+(d) zero, with `create_dir` actually rejecting a name twice in 80000, so layer 2 is not decorative. THE UNIQUENESS PROPERTY ITSELF HOLDS and both lenses verified it independently; the fix set below is about COVERAGE AND EDGES, not about the fix being wrong.

THE RED-3 MUTATION VINDICATED THE `risky` CLASSIFICATION. Prepending the disambiguator instead of appending it (`{seq}-{pid}-{nanos}`) breaks `owning_pid` parsing and makes the startup prune RECLAIM A LIVE WORKTREE, confirmed independently by the implementer, the evidence lens and the triager. The evidence lens found it STRONGER than reported: run in isolation a third prune test also fails, so the implementer's "one prune test still passed" was an artifact of which sequence values a run draws, not a property. That is precisely the destructive failure the classification was made for, and the fix now carries a named test pinning the pid's position.

BOTH LENSES INDEPENDENTLY FOUND THE SAME CENTRAL DEFECT, which is the strongest signal this round produced: NEITHER LAYER OF THE FIX IS PINNED BY A TEST (`T1`, `medium`, RAISED from the evidence lens's `low`). All four mutations reproduce: `create_dir` -> `create_dir_all` DESTROYS EXCLUSIVITY yet the whole suite stays green; the `AlreadyExists` retry arm is NEVER EXECUTED ANYWHERE, proved by panic-probing it and staying green; `fetch_add` -> `load` (a constant sequence) stays green 3 of 3; and deleting the reservation is caught only INCIDENTALLY by a cleanup `.unwrap()` whose message says nothing about reservations. So the suite pinned "at least one disambiguator survives", not "both layers do", in a step whose entire purpose is establishing those properties.

THE TRIAGER DISPROVED THE ADVERSARIAL REVIEWER'S OWN PRESCRIBED FIX, BY IMPLEMENTING IT. The reviewer proposed a 3-line test (assert the reserved path `is_dir()`, then a second `fs::create_dir` returns `AlreadyExists`) and claimed it would kill three mutations. The triager wrote that exact test and measured: it passes on clean code, and UNDER THE `create_dir_all` MUTATION THE WHOLE `checks::` MODULE IS STILL GREEN (30 passed, 0 failed), because it tests `std` rather than the production site and never drives the retry arm. Implementing it as written would have produced a GREEN ROUND THAT PINS NOTHING. This is the SECOND time in this task that a reviewer's proposed remedy was unsound and the triager caught it by MEASURING rather than reasoning; the first was step 92's `FN-2`. RECORD THE LESSON: a reviewer's diagnosis and a reviewer's prescription carry different weight, and the prescription must be executed and measured before it is trusted.

THE AUTHORISED FIX SET, four fixes all in `src/checks.rs`, verified single-site by the triager. (1) `T1` (`medium`): extract `claim_dir(path) -> io::Result<bool>` (7 lines, called from the reservation loop) plus a 4-line exclusivity test; the triager MEASURED this green on `b890c4a`, RED under `create_dir_all`, and RED under a panic in the taken branch, so it kills the mutations the reviewer's version left alive. (2) `T2` (`medium`, a REAL BEHAVIOURAL REGRESSION the evidence lens missed): `fs::create_dir` rather than `create_dir_all` turns a `TMPDIR` naming a missing directory from WORKING into a HARD FAILURE, because `git worktree add` used to create the leading directories, and the error lost all context (`No such file or directory (os error 2)` against the pre-fix `WorktreeSetup` message naming the operation and full path); fix by `create_dir_all(&temp)` before the loop with path-carrying error context, and correct the comment claiming the reservation "changes nothing downstream", which is false. Fixes 1 and 2 land in one edit. (3) `T3` (`medium`): Module Invariant B at `:37-41` and two comments restating it at `:324-326` and `:816-817` are NOW FALSE, because a SIGKILL between `create_dir` and `worktree add` leaves an UNREGISTERED directory the prune cannot see; qualify all three, and do NOT widen the prune to sweep the temp dir, which would give it authority over other repositories' runner directories. (4) `T6` (`low`): move two `fs::remove_dir` calls above the assertion loop so a failing fixture does not leak. ACCEPTED RESIDUALS, do not re-raise: `T4` (a bypass at the production call site stays green; the brief already settled that the grep is the proportional evidence), `T5` (the `WorktreeGuard`-before-add move is unpinned; pinning it needs failure injection into `run()`, disproportionate), `T7`, `T10`. DISMISSED: `T8`, `T9`. ONE OFF-BY-ONE citation was found in a findings file (`:439-440` where the sentence is at `:440-441`), not a dismissal ground.

HUMAN DECIDED (2026-07-30) where the three `agent-scaffold next` defects go: FOLD THEM INTO GATE 4, the validation-constraints step, rather than giving them their own step, patching only the missing increment, or deferring them past the rename. Ground: the false-completion bug is a false green of the same family as `Q-55`, and its fix IS a constraint (a step's declared `[[step.increment]]` set should cover what its own brief commits to, and `next` must not assert completion from a converged last increment alone), so one planner pass covers both and the rename gates stay at three. This is a scheduling sub-decision, so no `[[question]]` and no receipt.

STEP 93 `checks-runner-worktree-name-collision` WORK LOOP OPENED (2026-07-30), GATE 1 OF 3 FOR THE RENAME. RISK CLASS = `risky`, so 2 consecutive clean rounds converge. CLASSIFIED BY THE ORCHESTRATOR AT LOOP-OPEN per its duty under the convergence rule, and stated to the human rather than put to them, because the rule assigns this call to the orchestrator. GROUNDS, and note they cut AGAINST the "this project over-classes prose work" calibration recorded for step 90: this is CODE, not prose, and the failure mode runs through `WorktreeGuard`'s `remove_dir_all` on a shared path (`src/checks.rs:329-342`) plus a startup prune that decides what to reclaim by parsing the owning pid out of the name (`owning_pid`, `src/checks.rs:400-405`). A wrong name format can therefore make the prune RECLAIM A LIVE WORKTREE, which is destructive and not cheaply reversible, and that is the definition of risky the rule gives. The artifact is EXACTLY: `src/checks.rs` (the shared name generator, the `run()` call site, the three fixtures at `:1462`/`:1491`/`:1492`, and the four comments the brief names at `:72-77`, `:400-402`, `:789-790`, `:845-847`) plus the new property test. If it grows beyond that, the classification gets revisited EXPLICITLY. THE STEP NEEDS AN IMPLEMENTER, NOT A PLANNER: its plan review already converged over 4 rounds. THREE DEFECTS IN `agent-scaffold next`, FOUND BY ACTUALLY RUNNING IT (2026-07-30), and the orchestrator's own dogfooding gap that hid them. THE GAP FIRST: the orchestrator had been reading the plan TOML directly with grep and awk and had NOT been running `next` at all, so none of this was visible until the human asked whether the tool was still being dogfooded. Running the tool you are building is not optional; reading its source data by hand routes around exactly the surface a user depends on. (1) `next` RECOMMENDS A FALSE COMPLETION, the most serious of the three. It reports `workflow-driver / workflow-driver-stage1 ... state: converged, streak: 2/2 -> mark-step-complete` with the advice "mark the step complete, re-render, and commit". That advice is WRONG: `docs/plans/agent-scaffold.steps/workflow-driver.md:15` states "The committed near-term path is 0a -> 0b -> 1 -> 2", and `:20` describes Stage 2 (the typed `src/driver/` FSM engine) as committed near-term work that is NOT BUILT. The step is correctly `in-progress`. The mechanism of the defect: `next` infers completion from THE LAST DECLARED INCREMENT CONVERGING, and the plan declares only `stage0a`, `stage0b`, `stage1` as `[[step.increment]]` entries while Stage 2 is committed ONLY IN THE SIDECAR PROSE, with no structured entry. So the structured source is INCOMPLETE relative to the prose, and `next` reads the structured source and concludes the work is done. Had the orchestrator followed the advice it would have marked a step complete over committed unbuilt work, which is a FALSE GREEN of exactly the class the rename is being gated on, emitted by the tool's own advisory surface. (2) `next` OUTPUT IS 79503 BYTES, because it dumps the ledger RESUME STATE verbatim; the previously recorded measurement was 150145 bytes of which 799 was live, so the defect is smaller but unfixed and still unusable. This is the failure `resume-state-currency-signal` exists for, gated behind `Q-58`. (3) `next` WITH NO `--source` reports "no plan source" and "no plan steps found" and exits calmly, rather than discovering the plan or failing loudly, which is false-green-adjacent and rhymes with `Q-55`. CONSEQUENCE FOR THE VALIDATION WORK: defect (1) adds a constraint candidate that is NOT in the list recorded above, namely that a step's declared `[[step.increment]]` set should cover the increments its own brief commits to, and more defensively that `next` must not assert completion from a converged last increment alone. It also strengthens the case that the release gates were the right call.

THE DOMINANT LESSON OF THIS TASK, established independently in BOTH loops: EVERY FIX PASS THAT AUTHORS PROSE MANUFACTURES THE NEXT ROUND'S FINDING. Step 92 rounds 1 and 2 were authoring passes (47 lines added, then 68 rewritten) and both re-seeded; round 3 was constrained to DELETION-ONLY and manufactured NONE. Step 93 rounds 1 and 2 wrote MORE than the verdict prescribed and all the surplus broke; round 3 was constrained to exactly two deletions with a mechanically checkable diff shape (two files, two hunks) and an explicit escape valve ("if you see something else wrong, REPORT it, do not fix it"), and the planner used the valve twice. GOING FORWARD: constrain fix passes to deletions and single-token reverts where possible, give the writer a mechanically checkable diff shape, and always give it somewhere to put the improvement impulse other than the file.

ORCHESTRATOR DEFECTS THIS SESSION, recorded so they are not repeated. (1) I imposed a "property 4" invariant banning the words every/only/authoritative/exhaustive/all outside the COVERAGE block. That word list cannot distinguish a QUANTIFIER from a LOGICAL CONNECTIVE, and it caused a correct one-directional "normalize equal ONLY WHEN" to be rewritten as a false biconditional ("just when"). AMENDED, and the amendment is now live: the rule is SEMANTIC (no COVERAGE CLAIM outside the block); the word list is a CLASSIFICATION AID only and must NEVER trigger a rewrite; logical connectives in mechanical statements are EXEMPT; and the single expected exempt hit (`only when` at the `normalize_wrapping` site) is RECORDED so a reviewer does not read it as a regression. (2) My step-92 consolidation brief FAILED TO CARRY FORWARD a requirement round 2's triage had explicitly set ("narrow this text in the same pass"), which surfaced later as finding `RD-2`. A valid verdict lost in hand-off is exactly what the findings-file rule exists to prevent. (3) Earlier defects already recorded above: risk-class drift, triager worktrees built from the wrong base, and a ledger edit that rotted four line citations.

Q-52 DESIGN PASS DONE (2026-07-24): four worktree-isolated explorers (prior-art, multi-signal design, skeptic, Q-58 ablation design) wrote records under `docs/plans/code-value-audit.explorations/`. CONVERGENCE: advisory-only never-auto-delete (Chesterton's Fence); cheap-static-first, defer the expensive deletion experiment which IS the reserved `Kind::Mutation` module (`src/checks.rs:94-95`); "nothing noticed" is necessary-not-sufficient (candidate not proof, oracle-relative) so the report carries a "not evidence of absence" caveat; exclude CLI/output/pack contract + cfg-gated + FFI before a human sees it; churn x complexity ranks remediation, not deletion. RESOLVED divergence: the crate is a PURE BINARY (no lib.rs/[lib], all modules private), so no downstream Rust API and the skeptic's public-API false-positive class largely does not apply (only the CLI/output/pack contract to exclude), making the cheap static slice low-false-positive here. SHARPEST CUT (Minimal by default): clippy `-D warnings` + rustc `dead_code` already cover the cheap case free, so a heavier tool's incremental value is unproven; defer the deletion experiment behind an EVIDENCE GATE (measured count of clippy-passed-but-dead cases). Q-52 DECIDED (human, 2026-07-24): schedule the cheap static report as new step `code-value-audit-static` (86, not-started) and GATE the deletion experiment as new step `code-value-audit-deletion-experiment` (87, deferred, the reserved `Kind::Mutation` module) behind an evidence gate (measured clippy-passed-but-dead cases); exclude CLI/output/pack + cfg + FFI, carry a `not evidence of absence` caveat, never auto-delete; Q-52 status -> `decided`, folded into step 86. `optional-modules` increment 2 SUB-INCREMENT 2c-ii (`--staged` + the pre-commit hook) review (CONVERGED via HUMAN DECISION at the round cap, RISKY, 5 rounds; worktree-isolated). Task `optional-modules-inc2cii`, artifact: `--staged` (index-tree isolation via `git write-tree` -> `commit-tree` -> the shared worktree runner), the inert `.agents/hooks/pre-commit` asset (delegates to `agent-scaffold checks --staged`; off-PATH SKIP with a note, human/reviewer-endorsed), the `scaffold --with-precommit-hook` create-if-absent install, and a new `executable` manifest field. Built by an isolated implementer in worktree `impl/inc2cii-staged-hook`. The implementer self-caught a LOAD-BEARING bug via the functional smoke (Principle 6): git sets `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_INDEX_FILE` inside a pre-commit hook, which broke `git worktree add` in the exact hook `--staged` exists for; fixed by stripping the git env from every runner call. Classified RISKY (writes into `.git/hooks`, index isolation, the never-clobber guarantee). This artifact was HARD: the adversarial loop found FOUR distinct write-escapes across rounds, each a variant of the same class, and each fixed. Round 1 (opus+sonnet+separate triager): 9 valid (1 med + 8 low, incl. F1 a dangling-symlink write-through via `exists()`-follows-symlink, the missing `executable` doc, the initial-commit staged test gap); sonnet F-3 (delegate `set -eu`) triager-DISPROVED empirically. Fix `6e9359b` (lstat never-clobber, `HookInstall::Skipped` so install errors never fail the scaffold, tests). Round 2 (opus+sonnet): opus found a HIGH, `--with-precommit-hook` into a SUBDIRECTORY of an ancestor repo installed a broken hook into the ANCESTOR's `.git/hooks/` (repo-root guard added, `9ca7ca9`). Round 3 (opus): another HIGH of the same class, a git LINKED WORKTREE escaped (shared hooks in the main repo); the fix generalised the guard to the load-bearing invariant, the RESOLVED hooks dir must be inside the output dir (`ff45a25`), which subsumed the whole class. Round 4 (opus, exhaustive attack across every git layout): the write-escape class CONFIRMED CLOSED; one new LOW (R4-1, a preview/action mismatch for a fresh-init inheriting a global `core.hooksPath` outside the dir). At this point the artifact had run 4 rounds without a clean one (each found progressively less-severe valid issues, high->high->low), reaching the 5-round total-cap. ESCALATED to the human per the cap contract (options + recommendation, ledger as evidence). Human DECISION: fix R4-1, run ONE clean confirming round, accept, matching ceremony to the now-reduced stakes since the dangerous class is closed and independently confirmed. R4-1 fixed with an honest conditional preview label (`e101539`). Round 5 (sonnet, fresh): CLEAN, streak 1. CONVERGED by the human decision (not the default 2 clean rounds for risky). 168 tests, clippy clean throughout. MERGE: fast-forwarded to `e101539` (main unchanged during the step). Rounds logged (records 78-83, incl. the `escalation` record with `human_decision: decision`). `decision-receipt` (Q-44 pilot 1) review LOOP OPENED. Task `decision-receipt`, artifact the decision-receipt record + W4 on branch `impl/decision-receipt` (commit `70903bd`, off main `db3ecd1`): `src/metrics.rs` gains the `type:"decision"` arm (q_id/options/recommendation/chosen with the `chosen in options` cross-field constraint via `require_options`), the narrow `Decision` struct + best-effort `parse_decisions`, and the drift-guard extension; `src/workflow.rs` gains `w4_problems` (forward-looking, boundary = min q_id index among existing decision records, historical Q-1..Q-44 exempt) wired into `check_workflow`; `pack/instrument.md` documents the record; CHANGELOG + regenerated `--instrument` self-scaffold (`AGENTS.md`, `.agents/AGENTS.reference.md`). `src/plan.rs`/`pack/plan-template.md` byte-identical; default scaffold byte-identical; 183 tests, clippy/fmt clean; real `validate --workflow` still exits 0 (W4 no-op with no decision records). Classified RISKY (it extends the enforcement backstop, like `workflow-invariants`/W3) -> TWO consecutive clean rounds required. Round 1: two reviewers with different lenses and models (opus enforcement-correctness/adversarial `decision-receipt-reviewer-opus.md`, sonnet docs-schema-consistency/tests `decision-receipt-reviewer-sonnet.md`) + a separate opus triager (`decision-receipt-triage.md`). Both verified functionally against the built binary. 5 findings deduped: 4 VALID (1 high, 3 low), 1 INVALID. V1 (opus F1, HIGH, must-fix): W4's derived min-`q_id` boundary is UNSOUND, the triager reproduced a silent FALSE-PASS (decide Q-44+Q-45, receipt for Q-45 only -> Q-44's missing receipt slips through because it is below the min-boundary) and a false-flag; any receipt-derived boundary is circular, so the fix requires an independent DECLARED `baseline` cutoff (pulls the baseline primitive, already the intended shared W3/W4 mechanism, into this step; refutes the step spec's min-`q_id` candidate, now corrected in the `decision-receipt` step detail). V3 (opus F3) INVALID: W4 accepting a malformed-but-present receipt is the E11 strict/best-effort split working as intended (`validate_log` reports the malformed record in the same `--workflow` run, so the command still fails). V2 (opus F2 = sonnet F-2, low): the duplicated `QUEUE_FOLD_PREFIX` in `workflow.rs` vs `plan.rs` has no cross-check (drift would silently disable W4); fix by exposing/reusing one copy or an equality test. V4 (sonnet F-1, low, downgraded from medium): the non-array `options` error path is untested. V5 (sonnet F-3, low): `instrument.md` implies `recommendation in options` which is not enforced (docs clarity; NOT a code change). No high/critical dismissed, so no backstop. Round 1 outcome: NEW VALID (4: 1 high + 3 low), streak 0. Fix all four (V1 the blocker), then round 2. NOTE (log-handling deviation, to avoid conflicts while the worktree owns a `type:"baseline"` log change): this loop's JSONL `round` records are recorded in this narrative now and BATCH-APPENDED to `docs/metrics/workflow.jsonl` at step close after merge; the narrative is authoritative for counting.
